Logarithmic supertranslations and supertranslation-invariant Lorentz charges
Oscar Fuentealba, Marc Henneaux, and Cédric Troessaert

Abstract
We extend the BMS(4) group by adding logarithmic supertranslations. This is done by relaxing the boundary conditions on the metric and its conjugate momentum at spatial infinity in order to allow logarithmic terms of carefully designed form in the asymptotic expansion, while still preserving finiteness of the action. Standard theorems of the Hamiltonian formalism are used to derive the (finite) generators of the logarithmic supertranslations. As the ordinary supertranslations, these depend on a function of the angles. Ordinary and logarithmic supertranslations are then shown to form an abelian subalgebra with non-vanishing central extension. Because of this central term, one can make nonlinear redefinitions of the generators of the algebra so that the pure supertranslations ( ℓ > 1 \ell>1 in a spherical harmonic expansion) and the logarithmic supertranslations have vanishing brackets with all the Poincaré generators, and, in particular, transform in the trivial representation of the Lorentz group. The symmetry algebra is then the direct sum of the Poincaré algebra and the infinite-dimensional abelian algebra formed by the pure supertranslations and the logarithmic supertranslations (with central extension). The pure supertranslations are thus completely decoupled from the standard Poincaré algebra in the asymptotic symmetry algebra. This implies in particular that one can provide a definition of the angular momentum which is manifestly free from supertranslation ambiguities. An intermediate redefinition providing a partial decoupling of the pure and logarithmic supertranslations is also given.



1
Introduction

The possibility to perform “logarithmic translations”

x^{\mu}\rightarrow x^{\mu}+C^{\mu}\ln r

(1)

without changing the asymptotic form
g_{\mu\nu}-\eta_{\mu\nu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{r}\right)
of the metric was already pointed out in the early 1960’s
Bergmann:1961zz
. The physical implications of these coordinate transformations were shown in
AshtekarLog85
not to affect the definition of energy-momentum and angular momentum, implying that these were somehow pure gauge (see also
AARP95
). Related studies include
BeigSchmidt82
;
Beig:1983sw
;
Chrusciel89
;
Ashtekar:1990gc
;
Compere:2011ve
;
Troessaert:2017jcm
.

Besides logarithmic translations, logarithmic
supertranslations
where the coefficients
C^{\mu}
, instead of being constants, depend on the angles, have also been contemplated
BeigSchmidt82
;
Ashtekar:1990gc
;
Compere:2011ve
;
Troessaert:2017jcm
. But because they induce logarithmic terms in the metric and hence conflict with its
1/r
asymptotic decay, they have not been studied much. However, one might wonder whether this is due to a choice of boundary conditions that is too strict. The logarithmic terms induced in the metric by logarithmic supertranslations can perhaps be tamed. In other words, can one extend the formalism so as to accommodate logarithmic supertranslations as symmetries? Exhibiting all the symmetries of a theory usually sheds useful insight on its structure.

We show in this paper that it is possible to consistently include logarithmic supertranslations provided the coefficients
C^{\mu}(\theta,\varphi)
are submitted to parity conditions that we explicitly spell out. These conditions imply that the logarithmic supertranslations that are incorporated in our formalism are parametrized by a single function of the angles, like the supertranslations. This function is naturally decomposed into an even and an odd part (under the antipodal map) in our
3+1
presentation, associated with spacelike and normal logarithmic supertranslations, respectively.

A hint that logarithmic supertranslations could be included, and the way to proceed, are given by the study of gravity in five dimensions
Fuentealba:2021yvo
;
Fuentealba:2022yqt
. The “Coulomb part” of the metric decays in
D=5
as
1/r^{2}
, and in order to exhibit the full symmetry, it is necessary to include a term that decays slowlier (
1/r
) but takes the explicit form of a diffeomorphism transformation (“improper gauge transformation”
Benguria:1976in
). As we shall show, the procedure for including logarithmic supertranslations in
D=4
parallels these steps, with the Coulomb part behaving now as
1/r
and the improper gauge part involving the slowlier decaying logarithmic term
\log r/r
.

The logarithmic supertranslations provide an infinite-dimensional extension of the standard BMS group exhibited first at null infinity
Bondi:1962px
;
Sachs:1962wk
;
Sachs:1962zza
;
Penrose:1962ij
(for recent reviews, see

Madler:2016xju
;
Alessio:2017lps
;
Ashtekar:2018lor
) and later at spatial infinity
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
. This extension will be called the “logarithmic BMS algebra”. With the definition of the Lorentz generators that naturally arises, the logarithmic supertranslations turn out to transform in the same infinite-dimensional irreducible representation of the Lorentz group as the supertranslations (quotientized by the four-dimensional invariant subspace of ordinary spacetime translations), even though they are characterized by functions with opposite parities under the antipodal map. Logarithmic and BMS supertranslation charges form furthermore a centrally extended abelian algebra such that the logarithmic supertranslation charges are canonically conjugate to the pure supertranslation charges (corresponding to
\ell>1
in the expansion in terms of spherical harmonics, i.e., beyond the ordinary energy and momentum). One can take advantage of this property to redefine the homogeneous Lorentz generators so as to eliminate some features of the BMS algebra that have puzzled the relativity community ever since the BMS symmetry was discovered. These features arise because pure supertranslations do not commute with the homogeneous Lorentz transformations.

Three of these intriguing properties are:

•

(Angular momentum ambiguity) It follows from the non-vanishing of the bracket of the pure supertranslations with the homogeneous Lorentz transformations that the angular momentum transforms under pure supertranslations. This non-invariance comes on top of the familiar non-invariance of the angular momentum under ordinary translations, but there one knows how to define an intrinsic angular momentum in terms of Casimirs of the Poincaré algebra, which amounts to compute the angular momentum with respect to the center of mass worldline. A similar construction for supertranslations appears to be more intricate for the full BMS algebra.

•

(Soft dressing by boosting) The Poisson brackets of the homogeneous Lorentz generators with the pure supertranslations involve not only the pure supertranslations, but also the standard
4
-momentum
P^{\mu}

Sachs:1962zza
. Thus if one boosts or rotates a solution with
P^{\mu}\not=0
one generates in general a solution with non-vanishing value of some pure supertranslation charges, even if these are zero prior to the action of the Lorentz transformation. This has raised some discussion in the literature.

•

(No invariant Poincaré subalgebra) The fact that the Poisson brackets of the homogeneous Lorentz generators with the pure supertranslations involve the pure supertranslations implies that the Poincaré subalgebra is not an ideal. At the same time, because the pure supertranslations do not form an ideal on account of the preceding point, they cannot be meaningfully quotientized out to get the Poincaré algebra as a quotient algebra.

All these features can be eliminated by a nonlinear redefinition of the homogeneous Lorentz generators that involves the logarithmic supertranslation charges. This is because the logarithmic supertranslation charges are canonically conjugate to the pure supertranslation charges.

We explicitly perform the construction of these new Lorentz generators in our paper. Once this is done, the pure supertranslations and the logarithmic supertranslations have vanishing Poisson brackets with all the Poincaré generators. The decoupling of the pure supertranslations agrees with the ideas pursued in
Mirbabayi:2016axw
;
Bousso:2017dny
;
Javadinezhad:2018urv
;
Javadinezhad:2022hhl
along different quantum lines. In particular, there are some similarities between the logarithmic supertranslation charges and the boundary gravitons of
Javadinezhad:2018urv
;
Javadinezhad:2022hhl
which are conjugate to the supertranslations in that work. Our considerations are, however, purely classical, do not need any form of regularization, and fulfill automatically all necessary Jacobi identity required by consistency since we work with a well-defined Poisson bracket. Furthermore, the new Lorentz generators manifestly reduce on-shell to surface integrals at infinity.

Our paper is organized as follows. In the next section, we briefly review the standard treatment of the BMS group at spatial infinity
Henneaux:2018hdj
and recall why logarithmic translations (and a fortiori logarithmic supertranslations) do not appear in that approach.
We then provide in Section
3
boundary conditions on the canonical variables that include terms generated by the logarithmic supertranslations. The boundary conditions involve also a crucial strengthening of the asymptotic behaviour of the constraints which is worked out in Section
4
. The boundary conditions are then shown in Section
5
to keep the kinetic term in the action finite, a key property that enables one to use standard Hamiltonian methods. The description of the asymptotic symmetries and the derivation through canonical methods of their charge-generators are then successively achieved in Sections
6
and
7
. The algebra of these canonical generators is computed and analyzed in Section
8
. We show in particular that the pure supertranslation charges and the logarithmic supertranslation charges are canonically conjugate. We take advantage of this property in Section
9
to redefine the symmetry generators in such a way that the pure supertranslation and logarithmic supertranslation charges have vanishing brackets with all the Poincaré generators. We first give a general algebraic argument to show that this is possible and provide then the explicit redefinitions. An alternative nonlinear redefinition of the symmetry generators such that the brackets of the pure supertranslations with the Lorentz boosts remain non-trivial but do not involve the ordinary translations (“no soft dressing by boosting”) is also given. The concluding section (Section
10
) recapitulates our results and suggests various future developments. Finally, three appendices of a more technical nature complete our paper.

2
The BMS group at spatial infinity
Henneaux:2018hdj

We recall in this section how the BMS group (and not just its Poincaré subgroup or the bigger Spi group
Ashtekar:1978zz
) emerges as symmetry group of the theory (i.e., of the action and the boundary conditions) at spatial infinity.

In the Hamiltonian description of asymptotically flat spacetimes
Dirac:1958sc
;
Dirac:1958jc
;
Arnowitt:1962hi
, the possibility to perform logarithmic translations does not appear. This is because a consistent Hamiltonian formulation requires additional asymptotic conditions besides the mere requirement that (i) the metric should deviate at infinity from the flat metric by terms of order
1/r
and (ii) its conjugate momentum should decay as
1/r^{2}
,

g_{ij}=\delta_{ij}+\frac{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}}{r}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}),\qquad\pi^{ij}=\frac{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}}{r^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-3}),

(2)

where
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}
and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}
depend on the angles only, i.e.,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{n})
and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}(\mathbf{n})
, where
\mathbf{n}
is the unit normal to the sphere (
(n^{i})=(\sin\theta\cos\varphi,\sin\theta\sin\varphi,\cos\theta)
). For the action to be finite
1
1

1

By finiteness of the action, we mean finiteness “on the nose”, without (foliation-dependent) regularization. This enables one to apply straightforwardly standard theorems of the Hamiltonian formalism, in particular the connection between symmetries, charges and their algebra.
and the Poincaré transformations to be bona fide canonical transformations (with well-defined generators), it is necessary to restrict further the metric (and its conjugate momentum).

The conditions that have been proposed in the literature are either strict parity conditions on the leading orders of the fields
Regge:1974zd
,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}(-\mathbf{n})=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}(\mathbf{n}),\qquad\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}(-\mathbf{n})=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}(\mathbf{n}),

(3)

or parity conditions twisted by an improper diffeomorphism
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
2
2

2

Strict parity conditions stronger than those of
Regge:1974zd
have been considered later in
Ashtekar:1990gc
from a different perspective.
. By this, it is meant that one allows an odd (respectively, even) part in
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{ij}
(respectively,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{ij}
), but it must take the form of a diffeomorphism at order
\mathcal{O}(1/r)
(respectively
\mathcal{O}(1/r^{2})
). In polar coordinates
(r,x^{A})
, (
x^{A}
coordinates on the sphere), the diffeomorphism-twisted conditions read explicitly

\displaystyle g_{rr}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})\,,

(4)

\displaystyle g_{rA}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})\,,

(5)

\displaystyle g_{AB}

\displaystyle=r^{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}+r\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}+\mathcal{O}(1)\,,

(6)

and

\displaystyle\pi^{rr}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-1})\,,

(7)

\displaystyle\pi^{rA}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2})\,,

(8)

\displaystyle\pi^{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-3})\,,

(9)

where the leading orders of the metric and the momenta obey the conditions
3
3

3

In writing the parity conditions, we assume that the coordinates
x^{A}
transform as
x^{A}\rightarrow-x^{A}
under the antipodal map. If one uses instead standard polar coordinates for which
\theta
changes orientation (
\theta\rightarrow\pi-\theta
,
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\rightarrow-\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
) but not
\varphi
(
\varphi\rightarrow\varphi+\pi

\frac{\partial}{\partial\varphi}\rightarrow\frac{\partial}{\partial\varphi}

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle=(\bar{h}_{rr})^{\text{even}},

(10)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}

(12)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\,,

(13)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V)\,.

(14)

We have imposed the extra condition that the radial-angular components
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rA}
are zero, which insures that asymptotic Lorentz boosts are canonical transformations
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
. We have also used the fact that at the leading order relevant to the analysis, the diffeomorphisms linearize so that their finite form is identical to their infinitesimal one.

The functions
U
and
V
, which parametrize the improper diffeomorphisms to be included in the asymptotic form of the canonical variables, can be assumed to be odd and even, respectively, since their even/odd parts can be absorbed through redefinitions. In the canonical description, shifts of the even (respectively, odd) part of
U
(respectively,
V
) are proper gauge symmetries with zero charges. Finally
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}
is the metric on the round unit sphere and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
the corresponding covariant derivative. The indices
A,B,\dots
of the barred fields are raised and lowered
using the metric
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}
on the unit 2-sphere at spatial infinity.

The logarithmic translations are eliminated with either the strict parity conditions (
2
) or the twisted parity conditions (
4
)-(
14
) because they induce terms that violate these conditions
Fuentealba:2020ghw

4

4

This argument also eliminates logarithmic translations for the differently twisted parity conditions proposed in

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}
should also be even in that case, while logarithmic translations induce the odd term
\sim C^{i}n_{i}
.
.
The connection between this Hamiltonian result and the insightful work

on logarithmic translations is that the parity conditions imposed in
Regge:1974zd
or their weakened twisted form
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax

;

starting as above at

for the metric and
1/r^{2}
for the momenta (see appendix of

With the twisted boundary conditions (
4
)-(
14
), the asymptotic symmetry group is the infinite-dimensional BMS group
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Henneaux:2019yax
. The twist is essential since without it, the remaining asymptotic symmetries are just the Poincaré transformations
Regge:1974zd
. The supertranslations generate shifts in
U
and
V
, which must be therefore be allowed to take arbitrary values.

3
Asymptotic conditions

To include the logarithmic supertranslations, one must impose less stringent boundary conditions on the canonical variables. This enlargement of the asymptotic conditions must obey two crucial consistency requirements: first, the action must be finite for all allowed configurations of
g_{ij}(x^{k})
and
\pi^{ij}(x^{k})
, even for those which do not obey the original boundary conditions; second, the BMS transformations and the logarithmic supertranslations must be canonical transformations with well-defined (finite) charges.

Deriving a set of boundary conditions that fulfill these consistency conditions follows usually a trial-and-error procedure. Rather then repeating the somewhat zig-zag way in which the conditions were arrived at, we shall write them first and shall then explain their various properties as we check that all the consistency conditions are met.

As we indicated, the allowed asymptotic form of the fields must generalize (
4
)-(
14
) by incorporating, besides the terms involving
U
and
V
, terms corresponding to changes of the fields under logarithmic supertranslations. More precisely, one must twist the strict parity conditions of (
2
) by a diffeomorphism that involves now also logarithmic supertranslations.

By following a trial-and-error procedure, we arrived at the following consistent fall-off of the spatial metric and its conjugate momentum,

\displaystyle g_{rr}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\ln^{2}r\,h_{rr}^{\log(2)}+\ln r\,h_{rr}^{\log(1)}+h_{rr}^{(2)}\right)+o(r^{-2})\,,

(15)

\displaystyle g_{rA}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}+\frac{1}{r}\left(\ln^{2}r\,h_{rA}^{\log(2)}+\ln r\,h_{rA}^{\log(1)}+h_{rA}^{(2)}\right)+o(r^{-1})\,,

(16)

\displaystyle g_{AB}

\displaystyle=r^{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}+r\left(\ln r\,\theta_{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\right)+\ln^{2}r\,\theta_{AB}^{(2)}+\ln r\,\sigma_{AB}+h_{AB}^{(2)}+o(1)\,,

(17)

\displaystyle\pi^{rr}

\displaystyle=\ln r\,\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}+\frac{1}{r}\left(\ln^{2}r\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{rr}+\ln r\pi_{\text{log}(1)}^{rr}+\pi_{(2)}^{rr}\right)+o(r^{-1})\,,

(18)

\displaystyle\pi^{rA}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ln r}{r}\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}+\frac{1}{r}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\ln^{2}r\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{rA}+\ln r\pi_{\text{log}(1)}^{rA}+\pi_{(2)}^{rA}\right)+o(r^{-2})\,,

(19)

\displaystyle\pi^{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ln r}{r^{2}}\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left(\ln^{2}r\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{AB}+\ln r\pi_{\text{log}(1)}^{AB}+\pi_{(2)}^{AB}\right)+o(r^{-3})\,.

(20)

The twisted parity conditions for the metric coefficients are

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle=(\bar{h}_{rr})^{\text{even}}+2\tilde{U}=\text{even}\,,

(21)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}

\displaystyle=D_{A}U^{\text{even}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{U}-U_{A}=\text{odd}\,,

(22)

\displaystyle\theta_{AB}

\displaystyle=2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\tilde{U}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\tilde{U})=\text{even}\,,

(23)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB})^{\text{even}}+2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(A}U_{B)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U^{\text{odd}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)\,,

(24)

\displaystyle\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\tilde{V}=\text{odd}\,,

(25)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr})^{\text{odd}}-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\tilde{V}-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V\,,

(26)

\displaystyle\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}=\text{even}\,,

(27)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\,,

(28)

\displaystyle\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\tilde{V}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\tilde{V})=\text{odd}\,,

(29)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V)\,,

(30)

where
\tilde{U}
is even and
\tilde{V}
is odd. The function
V
is even (its odd part remains pure gauge), but
U
has both odd (as before) and even parts,
U=U^{\text{even}}+U^{\text{odd}}
, the even component being non trivial once the logarithmic terms are included. Finally
U_{A}
is odd
5
5

5

We initially allowed also an even part
(U_{A})^{\text{even}}\not=0
, but found that this part must be equal to
U_{A}^{\text{even}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}U^{\text{odd}}
, something we already implemented in the equations in the text. The “left-over”
U_{A}
appearing in the text have thus
U_{A}^{\text{even}}=0
.
. The reasons behind these parity assignments will be pointed out as we proceed with the various consistency checks.

Since the parity conditions will be used very often in the sequel, we recapitulate them in a table:

Variable

Even component?

Odd component?

\tilde{U}

Yes

No

\tilde{V}

No

Yes

U

Yes

Yes

V

Yes

No

U_{A}

No

Yes

We observe that
V^{\text{odd}}
is in fact pure gauge, i.e., one may carry it along and observe in the end that it can be gauged away by a proper diffeomorphism, exactly as in
Henneaux:2018hdj
. To simplify the computations we set from the outset
V^{\text{odd}}=0
. The same is actually true for
\tilde{U}
, which turns out also to be entirely removable by a proper gauge transformation, so that only logarithmic supertranslations with
\tilde{V}
are improper. We keep
\tilde{U}
at this stage however, since proving its triviality is one of the essential results of this paper, which we want to explicitly establish.

Note that most metric and momentum components have mixed parity properties due to the twist, except
(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr},\theta_{AB},\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA})
which are even and

(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A},\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr},\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB})
,
which are odd.

A few comments are in order:

•

Just as
U
and
V
parametrize the standard supertranslations, the functions
\tilde{U}
and
\tilde{V}
parametrize the logarithmic supertranslations. Why they are only two of them (where one would expect four) and why they are subject to parity conditions comes from the requirements of consistency of the formalism, as we shall see below. In fact, there are two “hidden” extra functions
\tilde{U}_{A}
giving the other two missing logarithmic supertranslations but consistency with the absence of leading logarithmic term in
\lambda_{A}
, which plays a central role in the integrability of the charges, forces them to be equal to
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{U}
, so that they are not independent. This was explicitly taken care of in the above formulas, which explains why
\tilde{U}_{A}
does not appear.

•

The parametrization by the functions
\tilde{U}
,
U
,
U_{A}
,
\tilde{V}
and
V
of the metric and momentum components
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}
,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
,
\theta_{AB}
,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}
,
\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}
,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}
,
\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}
,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}
,
\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}
and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}
involves some redundancy.

\square

Consider first
\tilde{U}
, which is even. Given
\theta_{AB}
(of the required form), the function
\tilde{U}
is determined by (
23
) up to a solution of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\Upsilon+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\Upsilon=0
. But the most general solution of this equation is a linear combination of the
\ell=1
spherical harmonics
Y^{\ell}_{m}
, and so is odd or zero. Since it must be even, we conclude that
\Upsilon=0
and that
\tilde{U}
is unique (for given
\theta_{AB}
of the required form).

\square

The same argument shows that
U^{\text{odd}}
is determined by
(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB})^{\text{odd}}
from (
24
) up to
\sum_{m=-1,0,1}c_{m}Y^{1}_{m}
. This ambiguity corresponds to spatial translations, which are Minkowski isometries and have no effect on
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}
(but do act effectively on some of the other variables).

\square

The functions
U^{\text{even}}
and
U_{A}
(which is odd) are determined from (
22
) up to a solution of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}u^{\text{even}}-u_{A}=0
. But the general solution of this equation fulfills
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\Delta$\kern 0.0pt}}}u^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}u_{A}=0
. We show below that such an ambiguity corresponds to a proper gauge transformation, so that the redundancy in the description of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
has a clear interpretation.

\square

The equation (
21
) can then be viewed as defining
(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr})^{\text{even}}
given
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}
, while the even part of (
24
) defines
(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB})^{\text{even}}
, once a choice for
U^{\text{even}}
and
U_{A}
has been made from (
22
).

\square

Similarly, one finds from the momenta relations that
\tilde{V}
is potentially determined up to a constant, but since it is odd, it is unique and carries no redundancy. By contrast, the function
V
is even and so is determined up to a constant. This ambiguity corresponds to time translations, which are Minkowski isometries. Time translations are redundancies for the asymptotic fields considered here, but they are not when one takes into account all the other fields.

•

The next terms in the expansions (
\frac{1}{r^{2}}\ln^{2}r\,h_{rr}^{\log(2)}
, etc) must be explictly written because they can potentially contribute to divergences and so one must check that they are harmless. They are generated when one performs logarithmic supertranslations (so it would be inconsistent to set them equal to zero), but their explicit expressions are complicated because the non-abelian character of the diffeomorphisms becomes relevant below the leading orders, and the finite form of the transformations is intricate. We shall only need the equations that these lower order terms fulfill as a result of the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints
\mathcal{H}\approx 0
,
\mathcal{H}_{i}\approx 0
, which in fact determine some of them completely in terms of the leading order variables.

•

We have written in the expansion (
15
) for
g_{rr}
the subleading terms as
o(r^{-2})
rather then
\mathcal{O}(r^{-3})
because there are contributions such as
r^{-3}\ln r
, which are
o(r^{-2})
but not
\mathcal{O}(r^{-3})
. The same feature holds for the other expansions.

•

We will show below when computing the charges of the asymptotic symmetries that
\tilde{U}
can be completely gauged away by a proper diffeomorphism. This justifies the statement made above that it would not be a limitation to set
\tilde{U}=0
, but - as also announced above - we shall refrain from doing so since we want to establish its pure gauge character.

•

It is interesting to compare the status of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
for the various boundary conditions.

\square

The strict parity conditions of
Regge:1974zd
do not restrict
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
, except that it must be odd. By a coordinate transformation generated by a
\mathcal{O}(1)
odd vector field, which is an allowed proper gauge transformation in that context, one can set
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
equal to zero.

\square

With the boundary conditions of
Henneaux:2018hdj
that allows for a twist by an
\mathcal{O}(1)
even vector field,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
is not arbitrary any more since this would conflict with the integrability of the asymptotic boost charges. It is restricted to obey
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}=0
(see appendix C of
Henneaux:2019yax
for a detailed discussion). This enables one to set it equal to zero by a proper gauge transformation
Henneaux:2019yax
, a permissible simplification adopted in
Henneaux:2018hdj
.

\square

Finally, with the more general twist involving a logarithmic supertranslation,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\not=0
because it transforms under such transformations (the
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{U}
-term in (
22
)). For that reason, one must carry
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
. As we shall verify, this does not conflict with integrability of the boost charges even though
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\not=0
in general, because of the presence of the additional asymptotic fields.

4
Asymptotic form of the constraints

In addition to the above asymptotic behaviour of the fields, we must impose that the Hamiltonian and momentum constraint functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\pi^{ij}\pi_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}\pi^{2}\right)-\sqrt{g}R\,,

(31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{i}

\displaystyle=-2\nabla^{j}\pi_{ij}\,,

(32)

go asymptotically to zero faster than what the decay of the fields imply. As we shall see, this is necessary in order to have finite Lorentz charges and finite kinetic term. This extra requirement is similar to what was found in
Henneaux:2018hdj
. Writing out explicitly the consequences of this faster decay is the purpose of this section.

The first step is to expand the constraint functions in the limit
r\rightarrow\infty
. We start with the Hamiltonian constraint.

4.1
Hamiltonian constraint

In order to compute the asymptotic form of the Hamiltonian constraint
(
31
), we need the asymptotic form of the spatial curvature.
Using the useful expressions of the radial
2+1
decomposition of the spatial metric given in Appendix
A
, we find the somewhat involved formulas:

{}^{(3)}R=\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left(\ln rR_{\text{log}}^{(3)}+R^{(3)}\right)+\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\ln^{2}rR_{\text{log}(2)}^{(4)}+\ln rR_{\text{log}(1)}^{(4)}+R^{(4)}\right)+o\left(r^{-4}\right)\,,

(33)

where

\displaystyle R_{\text{log}}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta^{AB}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\theta$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)\,,

(34)

\displaystyle R^{(3)}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\,,

(35)

\displaystyle R_{\log(2)}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=-\theta^{(2)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta_{AB}^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta^{(2)}-2h_{rr}^{\log(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h_{rr}^{\log(2)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}\Big(3\theta_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\theta^{2}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\theta^{B}\Big)\,,

(36)

\displaystyle R_{\log(1)}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=4\theta^{(2)}+4k_{\log(2)}^{(2)}-2h_{rr}^{\log(1)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h_{rr}^{\log(1)}-\sigma+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\sigma_{AB}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\sigma-\frac{1}{2}\big(3\theta_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\theta^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}h_{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle\quad-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{C}\,,

(37)

\displaystyle R^{(4)}

\displaystyle=2\sigma+2k_{\log(1)}^{(2)}-2h_{rr}^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h_{rr}^{(2)}-h^{(2)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}h_{A}^{(2)B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h^{(2)}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}^{2}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{B}-\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}

\displaystyle\quad-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle\quad-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}

\displaystyle\quad-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}+\frac{3}{4}\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-\frac{1}{4}\theta^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\theta+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta+\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\theta_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\,.

(38)

In the above formulas, which involve the sub-subleading terms in the asymptotic expansion of the fields (among which
\sigma_{AB}
), the variables
\{k_{\log(2)}^{(2)},k_{\log(1)}^{(2)},k^{(2)}\}
are the sub-subleading
coefficients of the fall-off of the 2-dimensional extrinsic curvature:

\displaystyle K_{AB}

\displaystyle=-r\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}\ln r\,\theta_{AB}+\frac{1}{2}\left(-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}-\theta_{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\right)

(39)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{r}\left(\ln^{2}r\,k_{\log(2)AB}^{(2)}+\ln r\,k_{\log(1)AB}^{(2)}+k_{AB}^{(2)}\right)+o\left(r^{-1}\right)\,.

(40)

A direct computation shows that the square root of the determinant of the spatial metric reads

\sqrt{g}=\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left[r^{2}+\frac{r}{2}\left(\ln r\,\theta+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)+o\left(r\right)\right]\,.

(41)

Taking into account the fall-off of the conjugate momentum given above, we then get that
the Hamiltonian constraint (
31
) behaves asymptotically as

\mathcal{H}=-\frac{1}{r}\left(\ln r\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}R_{\text{log}}^{(3)}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}R^{(3)}\right)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\ln^{2}r\mathcal{H}_{\log(2)}+\ln r\mathcal{H}_{\log(1)}+\mathcal{H}^{(2)}\right)+o\left(r^{-2}\right)\,,

(42)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\log(2)}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big(R_{\text{log}(2)}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{2}\theta R_{\text{log}}^{(3)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}(\pi_{\log}^{rr})^{2}+2\pi_{\log}^{rA}\pi_{\log A}^{r}+\pi_{\log}^{AB}\pi_{\log AB}-\pi_{\log}^{rr}\pi_{\log}-\pi_{\log}^{2}\Big]\,,

(43)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\log(1)}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[R_{\text{log}(1)}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{2}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}})R_{\text{log}}^{(3)}+\frac{1}{2}\theta R^{(3)}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\pi_{\log}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\pi_{\log}^{rr}+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\pi_{\log A}^{r}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\pi_{\log A}^{B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\pi_{\log}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\pi_{\log}\Big)\,,

(44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[R^{(4)}+\frac{1}{2}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}})R^{(3)}\Big]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr})^{2}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}\Big]\,.

(45)

It follows from the definition of the field
\theta_{AB}
that
R_{\log}^{(3)}=0
,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta^{AB}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\theta_{A}^{A}=0\,.

(46)

Thus for generic boundary conditions,
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{O}\left(r^{-1}\right)
. However, we will impose a faster fall-off, namely
\mathcal{H}=o\left(r^{-2}\right)
. Therefore we add to the asymptotic conditions on the metric and its momentum given in the previous section the additional requirement:

\mathcal{H}=o\left(r^{-2}\right)\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad R^{(3)}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\log(2)}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\log(1)}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}^{(2)}=0.

(47)

4.2
Momentum constraint

If one expands the radial component of the momentum constraint asymptotically, one gets

\mathcal{H}^{r}=\frac{\ln r}{r}\mathcal{H}_{\text{log}}^{r}+\frac{1}{r}\mathcal{H}_{(1)}^{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\ln^{2}r\mathcal{H}_{\log(2)}^{r}+\ln r\mathcal{H}_{\log(1)}^{r}+\mathcal{H}_{(2)}^{r}\right)+o\left(r^{-2}\right)\,,

(48)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\text{log}}^{r}

\displaystyle=-2\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rA}-\pi_{\text{log}A}^{A}\right)\,,

(49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{(1)}^{r}

\displaystyle=-2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr})\,,

(50)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\text{log}(2)}^{r}

\displaystyle=-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log(2)}^{rA}+2\pi_{\log(2)}^{rr}+2\pi_{\log(2)A}^{A}+\theta_{AB}\pi_{\log}^{AB}\,,

(51)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\text{log}(1)}^{r}

\displaystyle=-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log(1)}^{rA}+2\pi_{\log(1)}^{rr}-4\pi_{\log(2)}^{rr}+2\pi_{\log(1)A}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\pi_{\log}^{rr}+\theta_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rA}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\pi_{\log}^{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\theta_{AB}\pi_{\log}^{AB}-2\pi_{\log}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\log A}^{B}-2\pi_{\log}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rA}\,,

(52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{(2)}^{r}

\displaystyle=-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{(2)}^{rA}+2\pi_{(2)}^{rr}-2\pi_{\log(1)}^{rr}+2\pi_{(2)A}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\pi_{\log}^{rr}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\theta_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}

\displaystyle\quad-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rA}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\,.

(53)

Similarly, the fall-off of the angular components of the momentum constraint is
given by

\mathcal{H}_{A}=\ln r\,\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log}}+\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(0)}+\frac{1}{r}\left(\ln^{2}r\,\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log(2)}}+\ln r\,\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log(1)}}+\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(1)}\right)+o\left(r^{-1}\right)\,,

(54)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log}}

\displaystyle=-2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\text{log}A}^{B}+\pi_{\text{log}A}^{r})\,,

(55)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=-2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\,\,\,A}^{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\,\,\,A}^{r}+\pi_{\text{log}\,A}^{r})\,,

(56)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\log(2)}

\displaystyle=-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\log(2)A}^{B}-2\theta_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\pi_{\log B}^{C}-\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}\,,

(57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\log(1)}

\displaystyle=-4\pi_{\log(2)}^{rA}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\right)+\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{BC}

\displaystyle\quad-4\theta_{B}^{A}\pi_{\log}^{rB}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\left(\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\right)-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\log(1)}^{AB}+\pi_{\log}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+2\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\,,

(58)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-2\pi_{\log(1)}^{rA}-2\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A})+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}

\displaystyle\quad-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\,.

(59)

It follows from the expressions
of the fields
\pi_{\text{log}}^{ij}
that the logarithmic leading terms
indentically vanish,

\mathcal{H}_{\text{log}}^{r}=\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log}}=0
,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rA}-\pi_{\text{log}A}^{A}=0\,,\qquad\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\text{log}A}^{B}+\pi_{\text{log}A}^{r}=0\,.

(60)

Accordingly, the components of the momentum constraint go generically as

\mathcal{H}_{r}=\mathcal{O}\left(r^{-1}\right)
and
\mathcal{H}_{A}=\mathcal{O}\left(r^{0}\right)
.
However, a faster fall-off will
be necessary for finiteness of the formalism and we thus impose

\mathcal{H}_{r}=o\left(r^{-2}\right)\,,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}_{A}=o\left(r^{-1}\right)\,.

(61)

This is equivalent to

\mathcal{H}_{(1)}^{r}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\log(2)}^{r}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\log(1)}^{r}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{(2)}^{r}=0,

(62)

and

\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(0)}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log(2)}}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log(1)}}=0,\qquad\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(1)}=0.

(63)

Imposing that the constraints decay faster than what follows from the asymptotic conditions on the fields is consistent in that it does not eliminate classical solutions, for which the constraints hold to all orders. Furthermore, this requirement is compatible with the asymptotic diffeomorphism symmetries given below because (i) the constraints transform among themselves under diffeomorphisms (they are first class); and (ii) in each case we are imposing the same extra condition that the first four non-trivial terms in the expansion vanish.

5
Action and Finiteness of the symplectic structure

The action of General Relativity in four spacetime dimensions in Hamiltonian
form reads

I_{H}[g_{ij},\pi^{ij},N,N^{k}]=\int dtd^{3}x\left(\pi^{ij}\dot{g}_{ij}-N\mathcal{H}-N^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i}\right)+\mathcal{B}\,,

(64)

where
\mathcal{B}
is the integral over time of a boundary term at spatial infinity, which depends on the asymptotic form of the lapse and the shift and will be discussed below. We assume that the metric is fixed at the two time boundaries as appropriate for the “
p\dot{q}
”-form - here
\int d^{3}x\left(\pi^{ij}\dot{g}_{ij}\right)
- of the kinetic term. This is the form of the action needed for computing, say, the transition amplitude
<g^{(2)}_{ij}(\mathbf{x}),t_{2}|g^{(1)}_{ij}(\mathbf{x}),t_{1}>
. If instead of fixing the
q
’s at the time boundaries, one would fix the
p
’s, one would need to make an integration by parts in time to convert
p\dot{q}
to
-q\dot{p}
, i.e., here,
-\int d^{3}x\left(g_{ij}\dot{\pi}^{ij}\right)
. This is well known and not peculiar to gravity. Such changes of representations are not the object of our paper, which focuses instead on the boundary terms at spatial infinity.

The dynamical equations in Hamiltonian form follow from extremizing the action with respect to the dynamical fields
g_{ij}
and
\pi^{ij}
.
Variation of the Hamiltonian action with respect to
N
(lapse function)
and
N^{i}
(shift vector) yields the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints
\mathcal{H}\approx 0
,
\mathcal{H}_{i}\approx 0
.

We now prove the finiteness of the kinetic term with our boundary conditions, something which is not manifest since there are for instance terms like
\int dr\frac{\log r}{r}
.
For definiteness, we work in spherical
coordinates. Recall that the momenta carry a density weight, so the integration in spherical coordinates is just
\int drd\theta d\varphi\pi^{ij}\dot{g}_{ij}
, without
\sqrt{g}
. We write this integral as
\int dr\oint d^{2}x
, where the
2
-sphere integral
\oint\equiv\oint_{S_{2}}
involves functions depending only on the angles., i.e., we integrate first over the angles and then over
r
.

The ingredients for proving finiteness of the kinetic term are:

•

The (strict) parities of
\tilde{U}
,
\tilde{V}
and
U_{A}
, which are

\tilde{U}=\tilde{U}^{\text{even}},\qquad\tilde{V}=\tilde{V}^{\text{odd}},\qquad U_{A}=U_{A}^{\text{odd}}.

(65)

•

The faster decrease of the constraints, but for this step, it is sufficient to have
\mathcal{H}=o\left(r^{-1}\right)
,
\mathcal{H}_{r}=o\left(r^{-1}\right)
and
\mathcal{H}_{A}=o\left(1\right)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle R^{(3)}

\displaystyle=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}=0\,,

(66)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{(1)}^{r}

\displaystyle=-2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr})=0\,,

(67)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=-2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\,\,\,A}^{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\,\,\,A}^{r}+\pi_{\text{log}\,A}^{r})=0\,,

(68)

from which one easily derives

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}=0\,,\,\\

(69)

using the expressions of
\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr}
and
\pi_{\text{log}\,A}^{r}
in terms of
\tilde{V}
.

Finiteness of the kinetic term is demonstrated as follows. Inserting the asymptotic expansion of the fields inside the kinetic term of the action, one finds

\displaystyle\int d^{3}x\pi^{ij}\dot{h}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\int drd^{2}x\Big[\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r}\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}+\frac{\ln r}{r}\left(\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}+\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}+2\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{A}\right)

(70)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{r}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{AB}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{A}\right)+o(r^{-1})\Big]\,.

(71)

The
o(r^{-1})
are harmless since the integral
\int o(r^{-1})dr
does not diverge at infinity. The other written terms are potentially harmful and one must verify that they are zero. We examine in turn the coefficients of
\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r}
,
\frac{\ln r}{r}
and
\frac{1}{r}
.

•

For the first term in the r.h.s. of (
70
), we express
\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}
in terms of
\tilde{V}

using (
29
)
and get that

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}x\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\tilde{V}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\tilde{V})\dot{\theta}_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\dot{\theta}_{A}^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\dot{\theta}_{AB})\tilde{V}

\displaystyle\approx 0\,.

(72)

This integral over the sphere vanishes by virtue of (
46
). Hence, the coefficient of
\frac{\ln^{2}r}{r}
is zero.

•

For the second term in the r.h.s. of (
70
), we express the “log” momenta in terms of
\tilde{V}

from (
25
) and (
29
) to get

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}x\left(\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}+\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}+2\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{A}\right)

(73)
