\displaystyle\qquad=\oint d^{2}x\Big[\bar{\pi}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{AB}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{A})\tilde{V}\Big]

(74)

\displaystyle\qquad\approx\oint d^{2}x\,\bar{\pi}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}\,,

(75)

where the constraint equation (
66
) was used. By expressing now

\theta_{AB}

in terms of
\tilde{U}
, one finds that this remaining term becomes

\oint d^{2}x\,\bar{\pi}^{AB}\dot{\theta}_{AB}=2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}\right)\dot{\tilde{U}}\approx 0\,.

(76)

It vanishes by virtue of (
69
). Hence, the coefficient of
\frac{\ln r}{r}
is also zero.

•

Finally, for the
1/r
term given in (
71
), we
use the expressions (
26
), (
28
) and (
30
) for the momenta.
After some integrations by parts we get:

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}x\left(\bar{\pi}^{rr}\dot{\bar{h}}_{rr}+\bar{\pi}^{AB}\dot{\bar{h}}_{AB}+2\bar{\pi}^{rA}\dot{\bar{\lambda}}_{A}\right)

(77)

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\Big\{\big[(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr})^{\text{odd}}-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\tilde{V}\big]\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}+(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{AB}+2\big[(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}\big]\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{A}\Big\}

(78)

\displaystyle\quad-\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{AB}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}^{A}\Big)V\,.

(79)

The last sphere integral vanishes by virtue of (
66
). Furthermore, since
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}
is even and its coefficient is odd, while
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
is odd while its coefficient is even, the only term that remains in the integral of the first line is
(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}\dot{\bar{h}}_{AB}
. We transform this term by inserting the expression (
24
) and integrating by part, which yields (recalling that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$U$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
is odd),

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}x\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{AB}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{A}\right)

(80)

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}+(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A})^{\text{odd}}\right)\dot{{U}}^{\text{odd}}\approx 0\,.

(81)

This integral vanishes again by virtue of (
69
). Hence, the coefficient of
\frac{1}{r}
is also zero.

The vanishing of the
\frac{1}{r}
term, which is necessary for the finiteness of the kinetic term, actually forces the parity of the functions
\tilde{U}
,
\tilde{V}
and the form of
U_{A}^{\text{even}}
, if we assume that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}_{\text{odd}}
,
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}
and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\text{even}}_{rr}
are independent fields. Indeed, the
1/r
term reads, using the expressions of the momenta and the constraint (
66
),

\oint d^{2}x\Big\{\big[(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr})^{\text{odd}}-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\tilde{V}\big]\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr}+(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{AB}+2\big[(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}\big]\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{A}\Big\}\,.

(82)

Inserting the explicit form of the fields, i.e.,

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle=(\bar{h}_{rr})^{\text{even}}+2\tilde{U}\,,

(83)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}

\displaystyle=D_{A}U+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{U}-U_{A}\,,

(84)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}

\displaystyle=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB})^{\text{even}}+2(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(A}U_{B)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U)\,,

(85)

(where we allow a priori an independent
U_{A}^{\text{even}}
-term, not necessarily fulfilling
U_{A}^{\text{even}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}U^{\text{odd}}
),
we find that the above integral becomes

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}x\Big\{

\displaystyle-2\big[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}+(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB})^{\text{even}}\big]\dot{U}_{B}-2\big[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\text{odd}}\big]\dot{U}

(86)

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\tilde{V}+\tilde{V}\big)\dot{\tilde{U}}-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{V}\big(\dot{U}_{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\dot{U}\big)

(87)

\displaystyle+2\big[(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr})^{\text{odd}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}\big]\dot{\tilde{U}}-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\tilde{V}(\dot{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}}_{rr})^{\text{even}}\Big\}\,.

(88)

The line (
86
) vanishes by virtue of the asymptotic constraints (
67
), (
68
), which imply

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\text{odd}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(89)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB})^{\text{odd}}+(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB})^{\text{even}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(90)

It follows from line (
88
) that
\tilde{U}
must be even and
\tilde{V}
must be odd. The above integral reduces then to

\displaystyle-2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{V}\big(\dot{U}_{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\dot{U}\big)\,,

(91)

which vanishes if and only if

U_{A}^{\text{even}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}U^{\text{odd}}\,,

(92)

as we wanted to prove.

We have thus established the finiteness of the kinetic term in the action and hence of the symplectic form. Finiteness of the symplectic structure is a cornerstone of our approach because it enables one to straightforwardly apply standard Hamiltonian theorems and, in particular, momentum map considerations. Note that finiteness holds exactly, without having to perform any regularization procedure.

6
Asymptotic symmetries

6.1
Form of vector fields

Since our boundary conditions involve explicitly terms generated by logarithmic supertranslations and ordinary supertranslations, we expect them to be invariant under diffeomorphisms parametrized by vector fields
\left(\xi^{\perp}\equiv\xi,\xi^{i}\right)
combining both kind of transformations. Their exact form will be shown to be

\xi

\displaystyle=br+\ln r\left(\tilde{T}+\tilde{T}_{(b)}\right)+T+T_{(b)}+o(1)\,,

(93)

\displaystyle\xi^{r}

\displaystyle=\ln r\,\tilde{W}+W+o(1)\,,

(94)

\displaystyle\xi^{A}

\displaystyle=Y^{A}+\frac{\ln r}{r}\left(\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\pi_{\log}^{rA}+\bar{D}^{A}\tilde{W}\right)+\frac{1}{r}\left(\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}+\bar{D}^{A}(W)^{\text{odd}}\right)+\frac{1}{r}I^{A}+o(r^{-1})\,,

(95)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{(b)}

\displaystyle=\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2\right)^{-1}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\right)\left[b\,\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{AB}\right)\right]\,,

(96)

\displaystyle T_{(b)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,.

(97)

The function
\tilde{T}_{(b)}
is well defined because the operator
\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2\right)^{-1}
acts on a function with no
\ell=1
spherical harmonic component. The ambiguity in
\tilde{T}_{(b)}
, spanned by the linear combinations of the
Y^{\ell}_{m}
with
\ell=1
, will be shown to be a proper gauge transformation.

The independent parameters in (
93
)-(
95
) are:

•

the function
b=b_{i}n^{i}
, which parametrizes the Lorentz boosts;

•

the vector
Y^{A}
, which parametrizes the spatial rotations;

•

the functions
\tilde{T}
(odd) and
\tilde{W}
(even), which parametrize logarithmic supertranslations; however, as we shall see, the transformations generated by
\tilde{W}
are proper (zero charge);

•

the function
T
(even) and the odd part of
W
, which parametrize the familiar supertranslations;

•

the even part of
W
and the vector
I^{A}
(odd), which generate a new type of supertranslations; however, only the combination
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}I^{A}
is physically relevant in the sense that if
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}I^{A}=0
, these new supertranslations are proper (see below).

We summarize the properties of these independent parameters in the following table:

Variable

Parity

Nature of diffeomorphism

Proper or improper?

\tilde{W}

Even

Logarithmic supertranslations

Proper

\tilde{T}

Odd

Logarithmic supertranslations

Improper (except
\ell=1
component)

W^{\text{odd}}

Odd

Supertranslations

Improper

T

Even

Supertranslations

Improper

W^{\text{even}}

Even

New supertranslations

Improper if
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}I^{A}\not=0

I^{A}

Odd

New supertranslations

Improper if
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}I^{A}\not=0

The independent piece of (
93
) is thus
br+\ln r\tilde{T}+T
, which can be chosen freely (within the class of boosts for
b
, odd functions on the sphere for
\tilde{T}
and even functions on the sphere for
T
). The extra term

\xi_{(b)}=\ln r\tilde{T}_{(b)}+T_{(b)}\,,

(98)

must be added when
b\not=0
in order to make the boost charges integrable (or, what is the same, in order for the boosts to leave the action invariant, without surface terms at spatial infinity). Similarly, the written terms in
\xi^{r}
can be chosen freely, while the independent piece of (
95
) is
\xi^{A}=Y^{A}+\frac{1}{r}I^{A}
. The other terms involving
b
,
\tilde{W}
and
W
are included to preserve the boundary conditions on
\lambda_{A}
under boosts (
b
), logarithmic supertranslations (
\tilde{W}
) and supertranslations (
W
).

The need to add extra correcting terms to the “naked” parameters is not a surprise since it just generalizes to the logarithmic supertranslation case what was found to be already necessary in the Hamiltonian formulation of the standard BMS symmetry
Henneaux:2018hdj
. While the need for the correcting terms associated with the preservation of the boundary conditions will be made obvious in the next subsections, the justification for those associated with integrability will have to wait until Section
7
.

6.2
Transformations of the canonical variables

The transformations generated by the diffeomorphisms (
93
)-(
95
) define asymptotic symmetries if and only if they preserve the asymptotic conditions and leave the action invariant (up to possible terms at the time boundaries).

If the asymptotic conditions are preserved, the diffeomorphisms induce well-defined transformations of the asymptotic fields appearing in the asymptotic expansion of the canonical variables. We deal with this point in this subsection.

The action of diffeomorphisms
\left(\xi,\xi^{i}\right)
on the canonical variables is given by

\displaystyle\delta g_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\xi}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\pi_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}\pi\right)+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}g_{ij}\,,

(99)

\displaystyle\delta\pi^{ij}

\displaystyle=-\xi\sqrt{g}\left(R^{ij}-\frac{1}{2}g^{ij}R\right)+\frac{\xi g^{ij}}{2\sqrt{g}}\left(\pi^{mn}\pi_{mn}-\frac{1}{2}\pi^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2\xi}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\pi^{im}\pi_{m}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}\pi^{ij}\pi\right)+\sqrt{g}\left(\nabla^{i}\nabla^{j}\xi-g^{ij}\triangle\xi\right)+\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\pi^{ij}\,,

(100)

where the spatial Lie derivatives are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}g_{ij}

\displaystyle=2g_{k(i}\partial_{j)}\xi^{k}+\xi^{k}\partial_{k}g_{ij}\,,

(101)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\xi}\pi^{ij}

\displaystyle=-2\partial_{k}\xi^{(i}\pi^{j)k}+\partial_{k}(\xi^{k}\pi^{ij})\,.

(102)

One quick way to arrive at these formulas is to observe that they are certainly valid for diffeomorphisms that decrease sufficiently fast at infinity that their canonical generator
G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]=\int d^{3}x(\xi\mathcal{H}+\xi^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i})
without surface term is well defined (proper gauge transformations,
G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\approx 0
). The variations of the canonical variables are then just obtained by taking their brackets with
\int d^{3}x(\xi\mathcal{H}+\xi^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i})
, which yields (
99
)- (
100
).
Since these formulas are local in space, however, they hold true independently of the asymptotic behaviour of
(\xi,\xi^{i})
and can be used even if a surface term must be added to
\int d^{3}x(\xi\mathcal{H}+\xi^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i})
to get a well-defined generator (improper gauge transformation).

There might be extra terms proportional to the constraints in (
99
)- (
100
) when the parameters
\left(\xi^{\perp}\equiv\xi,\xi^{i}\right)
involve the fields – as here, through the dependent correcting terms added to preserve the boundary conditions and make the boost charges integrable – but since the constraints decrease very fast at infinity, these terms play no role in our considerations.

6.2.1
Transformations under spatial diffeomorphisms

We first consider diffeomorphisms acting on the equal-time hypersurfaces (
(\xi,\xi^{i})=(0,\xi^{i})
). One directly gets from (
99
) and (
94
), (
95
) that the boundary conditions are preserved and
that the successive terms in the asymptotic expansion transform as

•

Leading order:

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\theta_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\theta_{AB}+2\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B)}\tilde{W}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\tilde{W}\right)\,.

(103)

•

First subleading order:

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle=Y^{A}\partial_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+2\tilde{W}\,,

(104)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\lambda_{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W^{\textrm{even}}-I_{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{W}\,,

(105)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}+2\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(A}I_{B)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}W^{\textrm{odd}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}W\right)\,.

(106)

Note that the term
\frac{\ln r}{r}\bar{D}^{A}\tilde{W}+\frac{1}{r}\bar{D}^{A}(W)^{\text{odd}}
must be included in
\xi^{A}
in order to maintain the asymptotic form of the mixed component
g_{rA}
. If we had allowed in
\xi^{A}
the term
\frac{\ln r}{r}\tilde{I}^{A}+\frac{1}{r}I^{A}+o(r^{-1})
with arbitrary
\tilde{I}^{A}
and with
I^{A}
having an arbitrary even piece, we would have found the extra terms

(\delta_{\xi^{i}}g_{rA})^{\textrm{extra}}=\ln r\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{W}-\tilde{I}_{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W^{\textrm{odd}}-I_{A}^{\textrm{even}}\,,

(107)

which violate the asymptotic decay of
g_{rA}
unless
\tilde{I}_{A}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{W}
(to eliminate the
\ln r
-piece) and
I_{A}^{\textrm{even}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W^{\textrm{odd}}
(so that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}
is purely odd).

Turn now to the conjugate momentum. Under the action of
\xi^{i}
, the successive terms in the asymptotic expansion transform as

•

Leading order:

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\pi_{\log}^{rr}

\displaystyle=\partial_{A}\left(Y^{A}\pi_{\log}^{rr}\right)\,,

(108)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\pi_{\log}^{rA}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\pi_{\log}^{rA}\,,

(109)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\pi_{\log}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\pi_{\log}^{AB}\,,\,.

(110)

•

First subleading order:

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=\partial_{A}\left(Y^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\right)\,,

(111)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\,,

(112)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi^{i}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\,.

(113)

This follows directly from (
100
) and (
94
), (
95
).

6.2.2
Transformations under normal diffeomorphisms

The action of the “naked” boosts
\text{$\xi$}=br
on
g_{rA}
violate the boundary conditions. Indeed, it follows from (
99
) that
g_{rA}
transforms as

\delta_{\xi}g_{rA}=\ln r\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\pi_{\log A}^{r}+\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}+o(1)\,.

(114)

For this reason, as announced above,
one must perform a corrective gauge transformation
\tilde{I}_{A}^{(b)}=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 0.90417pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\pi_{\log A}^{r}
in order to maintain the condition
g_{rA}=\mathcal{O}(1)
.
However, the resulting transformation is non-canonical and even generates divergences in the charge (see section

7.2
below). To cure
this problem, the leading corrective transformation
\tilde{I}_{A}^{(b)}
must be accompanied by a subleading
corrective gauge transformation
I_{A}^{(b)}=\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 0.90417pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}
. Similarly, integrability dictate the need to include the corrective terms
\xi_{(b)}=\ln r\tilde{T}_{(b)}+T_{(b)}
(see same section

7.2
below).

The normal diffeomorphisms
\xi
with all the corrections included,

\xi

\displaystyle=br+\ln r\left(\tilde{T}+\tilde{T}_{(b)}\right)+T+T_{(b)}\,,

(115)

\displaystyle\xi^{r}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\xi^{A}=\frac{\ln r}{r}\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\pi_{\log}^{rA}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\,,

(116)

preserve the asymptotic decay of the fields and their action on the successive terms in the asymptotic expansion can be worked out to be:

These transformations preserve the parity conditions.

(126)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{T}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\tilde{T}+\tilde{T}_{(b)}\,,

(127)

\displaystyle T^{\prime}

\displaystyle=T+T_{(b)}\,.

(128)

It follows in particular from the above formulas that the transformation laws of the fields
\tilde{U}
and

\tilde{V}
under diffeomorphisms with both normal and spatial components are

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\tilde{U}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\tilde{U}-b\tilde{V}+\tilde{W}\,,

(129)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\tilde{V}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\tilde{V}-\frac{1}{2}b\tilde{U}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{U}+\tilde{T}+\tilde{T}_{(b)}\,.

(130)

7
Canonical generator

We now turn to the computation of the canonical generator
G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
, which takes the form

G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]=\int d^{3}x\Big(\xi\mathcal{H}+\xi^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i}\Big)+Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]\,,

(131)

where the surface term must be determined, following standard Hamiltonian methods, in such a way that
\iota_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\Omega=-d_{V}G
. Here,
\iota_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\Omega
is the internal contraction of the symplectic form with the phase space vector field
X_{\xi,\xi^{i}}
associated with
(\xi,\xi^{i})
and
d_{V}
is the exterior derivative in phase space. The equation guarantees
d_{V}(\iota_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\Omega)=0
, i.e,
\mathcal{L}_{X_{\xi,\xi^{i}}}\Omega=0
, which expresses the invariance of the symplectic structure under the phase space transformation (exactly and not up to surface terms at spatial infinity).

Because the symplectic form takes the standard bulk form
\Omega=\int d^{3}x\,d_{V}\pi^{ij}\wedge d_{V}g_{ij}
(without surface contribution), this amounts to applying the method of
Regge:1974zd
(see
Henneaux:2018gfi
for more information). Namely, one computes
d_{V}G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
and gets an equation for
d_{V}Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
by requesting that the surface terms obtained through the integrations by parts necessary to bring
d_{V}G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
to the canonical form
\int d^{3}x(A^{ij}d_{V}g_{ij}+B_{ij}d_{V}\pi^{ij})
be cancelled by
d_{V}Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
. Of course, for this to work, one must get a finite and
d_{V}
-closed (“integrable”) expression for
d_{V}Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
. In order to check these properties and derive the surface term, one can of course use the boundary conditions.

The surface term that one gets from the variation of the bulk part of
G_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
reads

-\oint d^{2}x\Big[2\sqrt{g}\xi\delta K+\sqrt{g}g^{BC}\delta g_{CA}\Big(\xi K_{B}^{A}+\frac{1}{\lambda}(\partial_{r}\xi-\lambda^{D}\partial_{D}\xi)\delta_{B}^{A}\Big)+2\xi^{i}\delta\pi_{i}^{r}-\xi^{r}\delta g_{jk}\pi^{jk}\Big]\,,

where we have made the change of notations
d_{V}\rightarrow\delta
to follow the tradition in the field.
It follows that the compensation condition that determines
Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
is

\delta Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]=\oint d^{2}x\Big[2\sqrt{g}\xi\delta K+\sqrt{g}g^{BC}\delta g_{CA}\Big(\xi K_{B}^{A}+\frac{1}{\lambda}(\partial_{r}\xi-\lambda^{D}\partial_{D}\xi)\delta_{B}^{A}\Big)+2\xi^{i}\delta\pi_{i}^{r}-\xi^{r}\delta g_{jk}\pi^{jk}\Big]\,,

(132)

where
K_{B}^{A}
is the extrinsic curvature of the
2
-spheres in a
2+1
decomposition, see Appendix
A
.

We shall now verify that with the above boundary conditions and improved diffeomorphism parameters, not only is the right-hand side of this equation finite but also integrable, yielding a finite, well-defined
Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]
.

7.1
Generators of spatial diffeomorphisms

We shall compute in great detail the generators of spatial diffeomorphisms to illustrate both the importance of the boundary conditions and the inner consistency of the formalism.
Inserting the variations of the fields under spatial diffeomorphisms in (
132
) yields

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\xi^{i}}

\displaystyle=2r\ln r\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\delta\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}

(133)

\displaystyle\quad+2r\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}

(134)

\displaystyle\quad+2\ln^{2}r\oint d^{2}x\left[Y_{A}\delta\left(\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{rA}+\pi_{\log}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\tilde{W}\delta\left(\pi_{\log}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rA}\right)\right]

(135)

\displaystyle\quad+2\ln r\oint d^{2}x\Big[Y_{A}\delta\left(\pi_{\text{log}(1)}^{rA}+\pi_{\log}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\tilde{W}\delta\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\right)+W\delta\pi_{\log}^{rr}+I_{A}\delta\pi_{\log}^{rA}\Big]

(136)

\displaystyle\quad+\oint d^{2}x\left[2Y_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(2)}^{rA}+2W\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}+2I_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\right]\,,

(137)

where

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(2)}^{rA}=\pi_{(2)}^{rA}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\,.

(138)

As it is manifest from this expression, the variation of the charge is plagued with four different types of potential divergences, which we must show are actually zero. It is here that we shall need the faster decay of the constraints.

We examine the potential divergences in turn, starting with the most “dangerous” ones.

•

The coefficient of
r\ln r
is zero by parity, since
\pi_{\log}^{rA}

is even while
Y_{A}
is odd.

•

For the term proportional to
r
, we use the asymptotic expression
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA})^{\text{even}}+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V
.
Taking into account the parities of the fields, the integral reduces then to

\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\,,

(139)

which vanishes under integration by parts by using the Killing equation

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}Y^{A}=0
.

•

For the term proportional to
\ln^{2}r
, we first observe that
the integral that involves
\tilde{W}
vanishes by parity (
\tilde{W}

is even). Using also that
Y_{A}\pi_{\log}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}

is odd, we therefore find that this term reduces to

\oint d^{2}x\,Y_{A}\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{rA}\,.

(140)

In order to show that this expression is zero, we use the assumption that the angular component
\mathcal{H}_{A}

of the momentum constraint goes to zero as
o(r^{-1})
. In particular,
from
\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log(1)}}=0
(coefficient of
r^{-1}\ln r
in
\mathcal{H}_{A}
),
we can relate the sub-subleading coefficient
\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{rA}

with the leading and subleading coefficients in the asymptotic expansion of the
fields. Indeed, the equation
\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\text{log(1)}}=0
reads

\displaystyle-4\pi_{\log(2)}^{rA}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\right)+\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{BC}

(141)

\displaystyle-4\theta_{B}^{A}\pi_{\log}^{rB}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\left(\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\right)-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\log(1)}^{AB}+\pi_{\log}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+2\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(142)

Now, all the terms in the second line (
142
) are even,
thus once they are inserted in the integrand
Y_{A}\pi_{\log(2)}^{rA}
,
they will vanish (under the integral). Hence, the integral (
140
)
becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\right)+\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\right]+\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{BC}\right]\,.

(143)

\square

Let us focus on the first integral

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\right)+\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\right]\,.

(144)

Using again parity arguments, we see that only the odd part of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}
contributes,
which is “pure gauge”, i.e.,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}^{\text{odd}}=2\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U_{\text{odd}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U_{\text{odd}}\right)\,.

(145)

Recall that
U_{A}^{\text{even}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}U_{\text{odd}}
. Thus,
by using that
Y_{A}
is a Killing vector of the 2-sphere,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(A}Y_{B)}=0\,,\qquad\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}Y_{C}=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}Y_{C}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AC}Y_{B}\,,

(146)

that
\pi_{\text{log}}^{AB}=\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\tilde{V}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\tilde{V})
,
and integrating by parts, we get that the first integral in (
144
)
becomes

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\right)\right]=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}Y_{A}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\tilde{V}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}\right)U_{\text{odd}}\,.

(147)

We also used the property that the commutator of two covariant derivatives on the
round 2-sphere implies
[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}]\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\tilde{V}=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{V}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\tilde{V}
.
For the second integral in (
144
), besides of making use
of exactly the same ingredients as before, we must also use the expression for the commutator of
two covariant derivatives of a (symmetric) tensor field, which reads

[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B},\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}]\pi_{\log}^{BC}=2\pi_{\log}^{AC}-\pi_{\log}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AC}\,.

(148)

We obtain then the equality

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}=-\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,Y_{A}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\tilde{V}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\tilde{V}\right)U_{\text{odd}}\,.

(149)

Adding the two terms in (
144
), we get that the first integral in (
143
) vanishes,

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\right)+\pi_{\log}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\right]=0\,.

(150)

\square

Now, for the second integral in (
143
),

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{BC}\right]\,,

(151)

the steps are very similar. We first note that (by parity) only the
odd part of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}
and the even part of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}

contribute. These read

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V_{\text{even}}\,,

(152)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V_{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V_{\text{even}})\,.

(153)

Making use of the identities
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rB}

and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta_{AC}
, the integral
(
151
) can be re-written as

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\left(2Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}-Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)\,.

(154)

It is easy to see that the first term of the above integral becomes
(after integration by parts and using the Killing equation)

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\left(2Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta^{AB}V_{\text{even}}\,.

(155)

The second term, on the other hand, can be written as (after integration
by parts)

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\left(-Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)=\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left[\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\left(Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\left(Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta\right)\right]V_{\text{even}}\,.

(156)

Expanding the derivatives in the above integral, using the identities
of the Killing vector
Y^{A}
and the commutator of two covariant
derivatives, we get that the above term reduces to

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\left(-Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta^{AB}V_{\text{even}}\,.

(157)

Thus, the integral (
151
) also vanishes

\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta_{BC}\right]=0\,.

(158)

Consequently, we have proven that

\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\pi_{\text{log}(2)}^{rA}=0\,,

(159)

so that the divergent term involving
\ln^{2}r
is actually absent.

•

For the divergent term proportional to
\ln r
, we proceed along lines that adopt similar arguments. For the term proportional to
\tilde{W}
, we use the constraint
\mathcal{H}_{(1)}^{r}\approx 0
in (
50
), to get

\oint d^{2}x\tilde{W\delta}\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\big)=\oint d^{2}x\tilde{W}\delta\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr}\right)\,,

(160)

Note that by virtue of the parity conditions of the leading coefficients of the momenta, the quantity

\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr}\right)

is strictly odd. Therefore, since
\tilde{W}
is even, the integral vanishes.
Consider now the terms involving
W
and
I_{A}
. These become

\oint d^{2}x\Big(W^{\text{odd}}\delta\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}+I_{A}^{\text{even}}\delta\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}\Big)=\oint d^{2}xW^{\text{\text{odd}}}\delta\Big(\pi_{\text{log}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\text{log}}^{rA}\Big)=0\,,

(161)

where the relation
I_{A}^{\text{even}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}W^{\text{\text{odd}}}
was used.
Thus, the total integral reduces to the term coming from the spatial
rotations

\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left(\pi_{\text{log}(1)}^{rA}+\pi_{\log}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)\,.

(162)

Since the second term
Y_{A}(\pi_{\log}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A})

is odd, it does not contribute. Thus, our remaining duty is
to show that

\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left(\pi_{\text{log}(1)}^{rA}+\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)=0\,.

(163)

As for the divergence proportional to
\ln^{2}r
, we will make use of the fast fall-off of
the angular components of the momentum constraint. In this case, we
need the equation
\mathcal{H}_{A}^{(1)}=0
expressing that the
r^{-1}
term in
\mathcal{H}_{A}
vanishes,

\displaystyle-2\pi_{\log(1)}^{rA}-2\pi_{\log}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\theta_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A})+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}

(164)

\displaystyle-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(165)

Replacing
\pi_{\log(1)}^{rA}
in (
163
), we find that this
integral becomes

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\right]\,.

(166)

In the first integral

\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\right]\,,

(167)

we see that only the odd part of
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}
contributes, which
is “pure gauge”:
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rA}=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V_{\text{even}}
.
Thus using the identities for
Y^{A}
and integrating by parts, we get that the
above integral becomes

\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\right]=\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}V_{\text{even}}\,.

(168)

The second integral

\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\right]\,,

(169)

can be split in two integrals as (by making use of the asymptotic form of the variables)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{\text{odd}A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}^{\text{odd}}\right]

(170)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{\text{even}A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}^{\text{even}}\right]\,.

(171)

For the terms in (
170
), we use the identities
for the Killing vector
Y^{A}
, the relation
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{BC}=-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{rB}-\pi_{\log}^{rB}
,
as well as

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}^{\text{odd}}=2\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U_{\text{odd}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U_{\text{odd}}\right)\,,

(172)

to show that

\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{\text{odd}A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}^{\text{odd}}\right]=0\,,

(173)

after integration by parts and some straightforward algebra.

For the terms in (
171
), we use the asymptotic conditions

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,V_{\text{even}\,,}

(174)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V_{\text{even}}\,,

(175)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V_{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V_{\text{even}})\,,

(176)

to derive

\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{\text{even}A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}^{\text{}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}^{\text{even}}\right]\\
=\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\right)V_{\text{even}}\,,

(177)

after some direct algebra and integrations by parts.

Putting together this result and (
168
), we obtain the required result that the integral

\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\left[-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}^{A})+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{BC}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}\right]\\
=\frac{1}{2}\oint d^{2}xY_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{BC}^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\right)V_{\text{even}}=0\,.

(178)

vanishes due to the asymptotic form of the Hamiltonian constraint. Thus, we have established (
163
) and there is no divergence proportional to
\ln r
.

This completes the proof that the surface term in the generator of spatial asymptotic symmetries is free of divergences. It is manifestly integrable and
given by

\displaystyle Q_{\xi^{i}}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\left(2Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(2)}^{rA}+2W\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}+2I_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\right)\,.

(179)

We note that
\tilde{W}
does not appear in this expression. This indicates that the corresponding diffeomorphism is a proper gauge transformation. One could use this proper gauge freedom to set
\theta_{AB}=0
since
\tilde{U}
transforms as
\tilde{U}\rightarrow\tilde{U}+\tilde{W}
under the logarithmic supertranslations generated by
\tilde{W}
.

7.2
Generators of normal diffeomorphisms

The equation for
\delta Q_{\xi}
reads, in the case of normal diffeomorphisms

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\xi}

\displaystyle=r\ln r\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\delta\theta

(180)

\displaystyle\quad+r\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\delta\left(2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\theta\right)

(181)

\displaystyle\quad+\ln^{2}r\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\delta\left(2\theta^{(2)}+2k_{\log(2)}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{4}\theta^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)

(182)

\displaystyle\quad+\ln r\delta Q_{\log}+\delta Q_{(0)}+\delta Q_{\xi_{(b)}^{i}}

(183)

where

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\log}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[2\tilde{T}\delta\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\right)+b\Big(2\delta\sigma+2\delta k_{\log(1)}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{2}\delta(-3\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}+h\theta)

(184)

\displaystyle\quad-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{4}\delta(3\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-\theta^{2})+\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\delta\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big)-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\theta\Big]\,,

(185)

and

\displaystyle\delta Q_{(0)}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big[\tilde{T}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2T\delta\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\big)+b\Big(\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2\delta h^{(2)}

(186)

\displaystyle\quad+2\delta k^{(2)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\delta\theta_{B}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{2}\theta_{A}^{B}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big)-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\Big]\,,

(187)

and where
\delta Q_{\xi_{(b)}^{i}}
is the contribution due to the correcting gauge transformation
that maintains the asymptotic form of
g_{rA}
. Using the formulas of the previous section,with

\xi_{(b)}^{A}=\frac{\ln r}{r}\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\pi_{\log}^{rA}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\,,

(188)

one finds

Q_{\xi_{(b)}^{i}}=\ln r\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\left(4b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{V}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\right)+\oint d^{2}x\frac{2b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}\,.

(189)

The corrective term
\xi_{(b)}=\ln r\tilde{T}_{(b)}+T_{(b)}
, necessary for integrability, has not been included yet since we want to show why it must be added.

The charge of normal diffeomorphisms possesses thus a priori the same different types of divergences as for spatial ones.
However, it is easy to see that the coefficients of
r\ln r

and
r
are zero by using the parity of the asymptotic fields and the equation

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}b+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}b=0
for the boost parameter.

We show in Appendix
B
that the remaining divergences also cancel. The computation is very similar to the one which we explicitly carried out in the previous section for the spatial diffeomorphism charges. Key in establishing the absence of divergences in
\delta Q_{\xi}
is the faster fall-off of the Hamiltonian constraint, which is part of our boundary conditions.

Once all dangerous terms have been shown to vanish, one finds that
\delta Q_{\xi}
reduces to the finite term:

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big[\tilde{T}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2T\delta\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\big)+b\Big(\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2\delta h^{(2)}

(190)

\displaystyle\quad+2\delta k^{(2)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\delta\theta_{B}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{2}\theta_{A}^{B}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\Big)-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\Big]\,,

(191)

While the terms proportional to
\tilde{T}
and
T
are integrable, those involving the boosts, which can be rewitten as

\displaystyle\delta Q_{b}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big\{b\,\delta\Big[2k^{(2)}+2h^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{AB}\Big)+\frac{2}{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}\Big]

(192)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\delta\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\Big)-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+b\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{AB}\Big)\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\Big\}\,.

(193)

are not integrable due to the second line.

In order to get rid of these non-integrable terms,
we perform the correcting diffeomorphism

\xi_{(b)}=\ln r\tilde{T}_{(b)}+T_{(b)}

announced above, to get that the boost charge is
