Q_{b}=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\,\Big[2k^{(2)}+2h^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{AB}\Big)+\frac{2}{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}\Big]\,.

(194)

The total surface term for normal diffeomorphisms then reads

\displaystyle Q_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\Big\{\tilde{T}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2T\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\big)+b\,\Big[2k^{(2)}+2h^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

(195)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{AB}\Big)+\frac{2}{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}\Big]\Big\}\,.

(196)

Collecting the surface integrals for spatial and normal diffeomorphisms, we conclude that the total charge to be added to the bulk term
\int d^{3}x(\xi\mathcal{H}+\xi^{i}\mathcal{H}_{i})
when the diffeomorhisms take the asymptotic form (
93
)-(
95
) is given by

\displaystyle Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\,\Big\{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\tilde{T}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T\big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\big)+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\,\Big[2k^{(2)}+2h^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

(197)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{AB}\Big)+\frac{2}{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}\Big]

(198)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad+2Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(2)}^{rA}+2W\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}+2I_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\Big\}\,.

(199)

We note - also as announced - that the
\ell=1
spherical harmonic component of
\tilde{T}
drops out from the charge and defines therefore a proper gauge transformation since the odd part of

h

annihilates it (under the integral sign).

8
Logarithmic BMS Algebra

8.1
Rewriting of charges

It is useful to rewrite
Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}
in a way that makes manifest the proper gauge transformations.
By direct computation using the asymptotic form of the fields, one finds that the canonical
charge can be recast as

\displaystyle Q_{\xi,\xi^{i}}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\Big\{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\,\Big[2k^{(2)}+2h^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{AB}\Big)+\frac{2}{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{r}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\quad\quad+2Y_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{(2)}^{rA}+2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T^{\text{even}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\Big)+2W^{\text{odd}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi_{\log}^{rr}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\quad\quad+2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T_{\log}U-2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,W_{\log}V\Big\}\,,

(200)

with

\displaystyle k^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sigma+h_{rr}^{(2)}-\frac{3}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+\frac{1}{4}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\theta

(201)

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}h_{r}^{(2)A}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\,,

(202)

where
T_{\log}=(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+2)\tilde{T}=\text{odd}
and
W_{\text{log}}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,W^{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}I_{A}^{\text{odd}}=\text{even}
.
Boosts, spatial rotations and supertranslations parametrized by
T^{\text{even}}
and
W^{\text{odd}}
are all improper, as in the case where logarithmic supertranslations are not included
Henneaux:2018hdj
. The new improper gauge symmetries arising from the extension of the formalism are the logarithmic supertranslations in time, parametrized by the odd function
T_{\log}
, which has no
\ell=1
spherical harmonic component, and the subleading supertranslations parametrized by the even function
W_{\text{log}}
, which has no
\ell=0
spherical harmonic component. These subleading transformations become improper and can be triggred because the condition
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}=0
is not any more necessary for integrability when the new asymptotc fields are introduced.

Note also that the ambiguity in
U
and
V
mentioned in Section
3
is indeed irrelevant for the charges, as it should.

8.2
Transformations of the parameters

The bracket of any two canonical generators is computed by evaluating the variation of one generator under the canonical transformation generated by the other.
One finds in this manner

\{G_{\xi_{1}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big],G_{\xi_{2}}\big[g_{ij},\pi^{ij}\big]\}=G_{\hat{\xi}}+\mathcal{C}_{\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\}}\,,

(203)

where the terms
\mathcal{C}_{\{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\}}
correspond to
central terms between the usual and the “logarithmic” supertranslations, and where

\displaystyle\hat{Y}^{A}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}^{B}\partial_{B}Y_{2}^{A}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}b_{1}\partial_{B}b_{2}-(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(204)

\displaystyle\hat{b}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}^{B}\partial_{B}b_{2}-(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(205)

\displaystyle\hat{T}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}^{A}\partial_{A}T_{2}-3b_{1}W_{2}-\partial_{A}b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W_{2}-b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W_{2}-(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(206)

\displaystyle\hat{W}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}^{A}\partial_{A}W_{2}-b_{1}T_{2}-(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(207)

\displaystyle\hat{T}^{\log}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}^{A}\partial_{A}T_{2}^{\log}-3b_{1}W_{2}^{\log}-\partial_{A}b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}W_{2}^{\log}-b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}W_{2}^{\log}-(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(208)

\displaystyle\hat{W}^{\log}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}^{A}\partial_{A}W_{2}^{\log}-b_{1}T_{2}^{\log}-(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,.

(209)

where from now on
W\equiv W^{\text{odd}}
.

In order to arrive at these formulas,
the following transformation laws were found useful,

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi.\xi^{i}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{A}^{A}\Big)=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{A}^{A}\Big)+\frac{b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\log}^{rr}\Big)+W_{\log}\,,

(210)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi^{i}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr}\Big)=\mathcal{L}_{Y}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{A}+\pi_{\text{$\log$}}^{rr}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+3b\Big]\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta_{A}^{A}\Big)-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T_{\log}\,,

(211)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi^{i}}U=Y^{A}\partial_{A}U-bV+W\,,

(212)

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi^{i}}V=Y^{A}\partial_{A}V-b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}U-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}U-3bU+T\,,

(213)

as well as the transformation rules of the sub-subleading terms given in Appendix
C
.

The relations (
204
) and (
205
) encode the homogeneous Lorentz algebra. The relations (
206
) and (
207
) indicate how the ordinary supertranslation parameters transform under the homogeneous Lorentz group, described in
3+1
Hamiltonian terms. They match the relations found previously in
Troessaert:2017jcm
;
Henneaux:2018hdj
.

Finally, the relations (
208
) and (
209
) define the Lorentz representation of the logarithmic supertranslations. As we have indicated,
{T}^{\log}
is odd and starts in a spherical harmonic expansion at
\ell=3
. Similarly,
{W}^{\log}
is even and starts at
\ell=2
. It is easy to verify that (
208
) has no
\ell=1
component and that (
209
) has no
\ell=0
component, so that these properties are preserved under Lorentz transformations. Since the minimum spin in the representation of
({T}^{\log},{W}^{\log})
is
\ell_{0}=2
and since the transformation of
{T}^{\log}
(respectively,
{W}^{\log}
) under boosts does not involve
{T}^{\log}
(respectively,
{W}^{\log}
), one can conclude that the other parameter
\ell_{1}
characterizing the irreducible representation in which
({T}^{\log},{W}^{\log})
transform vanishes,
\ell_{1}=0
(see
Naimark62
;
Gel'fand63
;
HarishChandra47
for more information). Thus, the parameters associated with logarithmic supertranslations transform according to the irreducible representation
(\ell_{0}=2,\ell_{1}=0)
of the homogeneous Lorentz group. This is the “tail”
Gel'fand63
of the finite-dimensional vector representation
(\ell_{0}=0,\ell_{1}=2)
of the ordinary translations. As it is known the supertranslations are in the semi-direct sum
(\ell_{0}=0,\ell_{1}=2)\oplus_{\sigma}(\ell_{0}=2,\ell_{1}=0)
, the tail being given here by the proper supertranslations (beyond the translations). The tail appears therefore twice when logarithmic translations are switched on, but it is realized by functions with different parities under the antipodal map. The ordinary translations appear only once, because the transformations in the finite-dimensional vector representation
(0,2)
are proper gauge transformations on the logarithmic side and are factored out.

8.3
Central terms

We close the discussion of the algebra by observing that the non-vanishing components of the central terms appear in the brackets
of the usual and “logarithmic” supertranslations. Specifically,
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\{T,W_{\log}\}}

\displaystyle=-\,\mathcal{C}_{\{W_{\log},T\}}=2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,T\,W_{\log}\,,

(214)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\{W,T_{\log}\}}

\displaystyle=-\,\mathcal{C}_{\{T_{\log},W\}}=-2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,W\,T_{\log}\,.

(215)

This is a centrally extended abelian algebra. It is easy (and instructive) to verify that these relations are compatible with the Jacobi identity. The opposite parities of
T
(even) and
T_{\log}
(odd) on the one hand, and of
W
(odd) and
W_{\log}
on the other hand, as well as the fact that these functions transform in (almost) the same representation of the Lorentz group, are essential for guaranteeing a non-trivial central charge and consistency with the Jacobi identity.

We note the important fact that the central charges vanish for ordinary translations in time (zero mode of
T
) and space (
\ell=1
spherical harmonics of
W
). This implies that the transformation rules of the energy and the linear momentum are unaffected by the extension of the formalism. In particular, the energy and momentum are unchanged if one performs a logarithmic supertranslation. By contrast, the angular momentum does transform under logarithmic supertranslations since these are in a non-trivial representation of the Lorentz group. This is of course the logarithmic analog of the fact that the angular momentum transforms under supertranslations, or even, for that matter, under ordinary translations. How to extract an intrinsic angular momentum (analog of the angular momentum “in the center of mass frame”) will not be studied here because a dramatic simplification of the algebra which bypasses this question can be achieved.

It is clear that the central charge is invertible in the remaining sector of the pure supertranslations and the logarithmic supertranslations. The relations (
214
)-(
215
) actually express that the generators of pure supertranslations are canonically conjugate to those of logarithmic supertranslations. More precisely, if we respectively denote by
S_{1}
the generators of the pure even
T
-supertranslations, by
S_{2}
the generators of the pure odd
W
-supertranslations, by
L_{1}
the generators of the logarithmic odd
T_{\log}
-supertranslations and by
L_{2}
the generators of the logarithmic even
W_{\log}
-supertranslations, the relations (
214
)-(
215
) read
6
6

6

In
S_{\alpha}
, the index
\alpha
runs not only over the discrete values
1,2
, but involves also the argument
(x)\equiv(x^{A})
of functions on the sphere, e.g,
S_{1}\approx 2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\Big)_{\ell>0}(x)
etc.

\{L_{\alpha},S_{\beta}\}=\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\,,\qquad\{S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}\}=0\,,\qquad\{L_{\alpha},L_{\beta}\}=0

(216)

with

(\sigma_{\alpha\beta})=\begin{pmatrix}0&-2I^{\text{even}}\\
2I^{\text{odd}}&0\end{pmatrix}\,.

(217)

Here
I^{\text{even}}
is the unit operator in the space of even functions on the sphere,

I^{\text{even}}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\delta^{(2)}(x-x^{\prime})+\delta^{(2)}(x+x^{\prime})\Big)

(218)

while
I^{\text{odd}}
is the unit operator in the space of odd functions on the sphere,

I^{\text{odd}}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\delta^{(2)}(x-x^{\prime})-\delta^{(2)}(x+x^{\prime})\Big)\,.

(219)

The Jacobi identity involving one Lorentz generator, one
S_{\alpha}
and one
L_{\alpha}
is equivalent to the invariance of the bilinear form
\sigma_{\alpha\beta}
under Lorentz transformations.

9
Decoupling of the pure supertranslations from the Poincaré generators in the logarithmic BMS algebra

In this section, we will show how we can take advantage of the central charge to redefine the Lorentz generators such that their action on the pure and logarithmic supertranslations is trivial. This decoupling of the supertranslations from the Poincaré algebra is performed by exploiting the fact just observed that the pure and logarithmic supertranslations are canonically conjugate.

First, we prove that it is possible to achieve this goal on general grounds, using general algebraic considerations. We then proceed to display the explicit computation of the realization of the decoupling mechanism.

9.1
General algebraic considerations

The logarithmic BMS algebra found in the previous section has the following structure

\displaystyle\{M_{a},M_{b}\}

\displaystyle=f_{ab}^{c}M_{c}\,,

(220)

\displaystyle\{M_{a},T_{i}\}

\displaystyle=R_{ai}^{\phantom{ai}j}T_{j}\,,

(221)

\displaystyle\{M_{a},S_{\alpha}\}

\displaystyle=G_{a\alpha}^{\phantom{a\alpha}i}T_{i}+G_{a\alpha}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\beta}S_{\beta}\,,

(222)

\displaystyle\{M_{a},L^{\alpha}\}

\displaystyle=-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\alpha}L^{\beta}\,,

(223)

\displaystyle\{L^{\alpha},S_{\beta}\}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\,,

(224)

where
M_{a}
,
T_{i}
,
S_{\alpha}
and
L^{\beta}
are respectively the generators of the Lorentz transformations, of the standard translations, of the pure supertranslations and of the logarithmic supertranslations.

Before proceeding, a word of explanation concerning (
223
) and (
224
). As we have seen, the generators of logarithmic supertranslations
L_{\alpha}
transform in the same representation as the
S_{\alpha}
(modulo the standard translations), i.e.,
\{M_{a},L_{\alpha}\}=G_{a\alpha}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\beta}L_{\beta}
. They also fulfill
\{L_{\alpha},S_{\beta}\}=\sigma_{\alpha\beta}
. We define
L^{\alpha}=\sigma^{\alpha\beta}L_{\beta}
, where
\sigma^{\alpha\beta}\sigma_{\beta\gamma}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\gamma}
(inverse of
\sigma_{\alpha\beta}
). Then (
224
) follows immediately from the definitions, while (
223
) is an immediate consequence of the Lorentz invariance of
\sigma_{\alpha\beta}
,

\delta_{a}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\equiv G_{a\alpha}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\gamma}\sigma_{\gamma\beta}+G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\beta}\gamma}\sigma_{\alpha\gamma}=0\,.

(225)

We now redefine the Lorentz generators by adding new terms as follows,

\displaystyle\tilde{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{a}-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}i}L^{\beta}T_{i}-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\gamma}L^{\beta}S_{\gamma}

(226)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{a}-L^{\beta}\{M_{a},S_{\beta}\}\,.

(227)

The extra terms have been added in order to trivialise the action of the Lorentz algebra on pure supertranslations by leveraging the non-zero bracket between
L^{\alpha}
and
S_{\beta}
. One easily shows that the action of the new Lorentz generators
\tilde{M}_{a}
on both pure and logarithmic supertranslations vanishes

\{\tilde{M}_{a},S_{\alpha}\}=\{\tilde{M}_{a},L^{\alpha}\}=0\,,

(228)

while the bracket
\{\tilde{M}_{a},T_{i}\}
does not suffer any modification. The redefinition (
227
) of the homogeneous Lorentz generators is bilinear in the generators of the inhomogeneous logarithmic BMS algebra
T_{i},S_{\alpha},L_{\alpha}
. The corresponding transformations differ from the original Lorentz transformations by a field-dependent logarithmic supertranslation, a field-dependent translation and a field-dependent pure supertranslation,

\displaystyle\hskip-31.2982pt\tilde{\delta}_{a}F=\{F,\tilde{M}_{a}\}

\displaystyle\hskip-25.6073pt=\delta_{a}F+(-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}i}T_{i}-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\gamma}S_{\gamma})\{F,L^{\beta}\}+(-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}i}L^{\beta})\{F,T_{i}\}+(-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\gamma}L^{\beta})\{F,S_{\gamma}\}

(229)

which, together, constitute by construction a canonical transformation (i.e., have an integrable, well-defined, canonical generator).

One also easily verifies that the Poincaré subalgebra is invariant under this redefinition of the Lorentz generators

\{\tilde{M}_{a},\tilde{M}_{b}\}=f_{ab}^{c}\tilde{M}_{c}\quad,\quad\{\tilde{M}_{a},T_{i}\}=R_{ai}^{\phantom{ai}j}T_{j}\,.

(230)

The modification of
M_{a}
given in Eq. (
227
) is the most drastic option. There exists a milder version that also decouples the pure supertranslations from the translations without trivialising the action of the Lorentz algebra on the former,

\mathcal{M}_{a}=M_{a}-G_{a\beta}^{\phantom{a\alpha}i}L^{\beta}T_{i}\,.

(231)

With this alternative redefinition, only the contribution from the standard translations
T_{i}
is removed from the right hand side of (
222
):

\{\mathcal{M}_{a},S_{\alpha}\}=G_{a\alpha}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\beta}S_{\beta}\,,

(232)

while all other Poisson brackets remain invariant. Both pure and logarithmic supertranslations
S_{\alpha}
,
L_{\alpha}
transform under the non-trivial infinite-dimensional Lorentz representation characterized by
G_{a\alpha}^{\phantom{a\alpha}\beta}
(or its dual for
L^{\alpha}
). Therefore, with this redefinition, one cannot induce soft hair by boosting a solution with no soft hair and with
P^{\mu}\not=0
, but the angular momentum “ambiguity” remains.

9.2
Explicit computations

9.2.1
New Lorentz transformations and charges

We now provide the detailed formulas. According to the derivations of the previous subsection, the combinations of field-dependent logarithmic supertranslations, field-dependent translations and field-dependent pure supertranslations that compensate the action of the Lorentz transformations on pure supertranslations and logarithmic supertranslations are explicitly described by vector fields asymptotically given by

\displaystyle W_{\log}^{(b,Y)}

\displaystyle=-\Big[\frac{b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})\Big]_{\ell\geq 2}-\Big[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta)\Big]_{\ell\geq 2}\,,

(233)

\displaystyle T_{\log}^{(b,Y)}

\displaystyle=\Big[\left(3b+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta)\Big]_{\ell\geq 3}+\Big[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})\Big]_{\ell\geq 3}\,,

(234)

\displaystyle W^{(b,Y)}

\displaystyle=b(V)_{\ell\geq 2}-\mathcal{L}_{Y}(U)_{\ell\geq 3}\,,

(235)

\displaystyle T^{(b,Y)}

\displaystyle=\left(3b+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(U)_{\ell\geq 3}-\mathcal{L}_{Y}(V)_{\ell\geq 2}\,.

(236)

Here the notation
\ell\geq n
means that in the spherical harmonic expansion of the functions, only the modes with
\ell
greater than or equal to
n
are considered.

The charges associated with these vector fields are integrable and well-defined and read

\displaystyle Q^{\text{extra}}_{b}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\Big[2b(V)_{\ell\geq 2}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\Big)\left(3b+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(U)_{\ell\geq 3}\Big]\,,

(237)

\displaystyle Q^{\text{extra}}_{Y}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\Big[2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\mathcal{L}_{Y}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\Big)(V)_{\ell\geq 2}+2\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})(U)_{\ell\geq 3}\Big]\,.

(238)

The new Lorentz generators
\tilde{Q}_{b}
and
\tilde{Q}_{Y}
are obtained by adding these extra terms to the original generators
Q_{b}
and
Q_{Y}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{b}

\displaystyle=Q_{b}+Q^{\text{extra}}_{b}\,,

(239)

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{Y}

\displaystyle=Q_{Y}+Q^{\text{extra}}_{Y}\,.

(240)

These boundary terms must of course be supplemented by weakly vanishing bulk terms with vector field
\xi^{\mu}_{(b,Y)}=(\xi^{\perp}_{(b,Y)},\xi^{i}_{(b,Y)})
having the given asymptotic behavior.

9.2.2
Poisson brackets of Lorentz charges with all supertranslations

A direct computation shows that these new Lorentz charges possess indeed the advertised property of acting non-trivially only on pure translations. The Poisson brackets of Lorentz boosts with supertranslations take the form

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{b},Q_{T}\}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{2}x\Big[-b(T)_{\ell=0}\Big](\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})=Q_{\hat{W}}\,,

(241)

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{b},Q_{W}\}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[-\left(3b+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(W)_{\ell=1}\Big]\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\Big)=Q_{\hat{T}}\,,

(242)

while the Poisson brackets of spatial rotations with supertranslations are given by

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{Y},Q_{T}\}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(243)

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{Y},Q_{W}\}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{2}x\Big[Y^{A}\partial_{A}(W)_{\ell=1}\Big](\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})=Q_{\hat{W}}\,.

(244)

We can read off the new algebra of the parameters:

\hat{W}=Y^{A}\partial_{A}(W)_{\ell=1}-b(T)_{\ell=0},\qquad\hat{T}=\left(3b+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(W)_{\ell=1}\,,

(245)

where we see that only the contributions from the translations are left.
Similarly, one can show that the Poisson brackets of all Lorentz charges with logarithmic supertranslations identically vanish

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{b},Q_{T_{\log}}\}=\{\tilde{Q}_{b},Q_{W_{\log}}\}=0\,,

(246)

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{Y},Q_{T_{\log}}\}=\{\tilde{Q}_{Y},Q_{W_{\log}}\}=0\,.

(247)

9.2.3
Poisson brackets of new Lorentz charges

We explicitly verify in this subsection the key property of our construction, namely, that the redefinitions of the Lorentz charges also leave the homogeneous Lorentz subalgebra invariant.

For this computation it will be useful to have at hand the new action of the Lorentz transformations on the fields:

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{b,Y}\Pi

\displaystyle=\Big[\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(3b\mathcal{T}+\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\mathcal{T}+b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\mathcal{T})\Big]_{\ell=1}+\Big[\mathcal{L}_{Y}\Pi\Big]_{\ell=1}\,,

(248)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{b,Y}\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle=\Big[\frac{b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Pi\Big]_{\ell=0}+\Big[\mathcal{L}_{Y}\mathcal{T}\Big]_{\ell=0}\,,

(249)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{b,Y}U

\displaystyle=-b(V)_{l=0}+Y^{A}\partial_{A}(U)_{\ell=1}\,,

(250)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{b,Y}V

\displaystyle=-b(U)_{\ell=1}-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(U)_{\ell=1}\,,

(251)

where, for convenience, we have defined

\Pi\equiv\overline{\pi}^{rr}-\overline{\pi}+\pi^{rr}_{\log}\,,\qquad\mathcal{T}\equiv\overline{h}_{rr}+\overline{D}_{A}\overline{\lambda}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}\theta\,.

(252)

We will compute the Poisson brackets of the new Lorentz generators case by case.

•

We start with the computation of the Poisson bracket between two Lorentz boosts:

\{\tilde{Q}_{b_{1}},\tilde{Q}_{b_{2}}\}=\tilde{\delta}_{b_{2}}\tilde{Q}_{b_{1}}=\tilde{\delta}_{b_{2}}Q_{b_{1}}+\tilde{\delta}_{b_{2}}Q^{\text{extra}}_{b_{1}}\,.

(253)

The last term in the above expression can be directly obtained using the new transformation laws of the fields:

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{b_{2}}Q^{\text{extra}}_{b_{1}}

\displaystyle=\oint d^{2}x\Big\{2\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b_{1}(V)_{\ell\geq 2}\Big[3b_{2}\mathcal{T}+\partial_{A}b_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\mathcal{T}+b_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\mathcal{T}\Big]_{\ell=1}

(254)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+2\left(3b_{1}+\partial_{A}b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(U)_{\ell\geq 3}\Big[b_{2}\Pi\Big]_{\ell=0}\Big\}\,.

(255)

The first term on the right hand side of (
253
) must be carefully computed taking into account the field-dependent gauge parameters given at the beginning of this section:

\tilde{\delta}_{b_{2}}Q_{b_{1}}=\delta_{b_{2}}Q_{b_{1}}+\delta_{W^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}+\delta_{T^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}+\delta_{W_{\text{log}}^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}+\delta_{T_{\text{log}}^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}\,.

(256)

The second and fifth terms give

\displaystyle\delta_{W^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}+\delta_{T_{\text{log}}^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}=

\displaystyle 2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}(b_{2}\partial_{A}b_{1}-b_{1}\partial_{A}b_{2})\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(V)_{\ell\geq 2}\mathcal{T}

(257)

\displaystyle-2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b_{1}(V)_{\ell\geq 2}\Big[3b_{2}\mathcal{T}+\partial_{A}b_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\mathcal{T}+b_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\mathcal{T}\Big]_{\ell=1}\,,

(258)

while the third and fourth terms reduce to

\displaystyle\delta_{T^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}+\delta_{W_{\text{log}}^{(b_{2})}}Q_{b_{1}}=

\displaystyle 2\oint d^{2}x(b_{2}\partial_{A}b_{1}-b_{1}\partial_{A}b_{2})\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}(U)_{\ell\geq 3}\Pi

(259)

\displaystyle-2\oint d^{2}x\left(3b_{1}+\partial_{A}b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}+b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)(U)_{\ell\geq 3}\Big[b_{2}\Pi\Big]_{\ell=0}\,.

(260)

Thus, putting all the terms together we obtain the expected result

\{\tilde{Q}_{b_{1}},\tilde{Q}_{b_{2}}\}=Q_{\hat{Y}}+Q^{\text{extra}}_{\hat{Y}}=\tilde{Q}_{\hat{Y}}\,,

(261)

where we used the bracket of the original generators,
\delta_{b_{2}}Q_{b_{1}}=Q_{\hat{Y}}
, and

\hat{Y}^{A}=b_{1}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b_{2}-b_{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}b_{1}\,.

(262)

•

We continue with the computation of the Poisson bracket between two spatial rotations:

\{\tilde{Q}_{Y_{1}},\tilde{Q}_{Y_{2}}\}=\tilde{\delta}_{Y_{2}}\tilde{Q}_{Y_{1}}=\tilde{\delta}_{Y_{2}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\tilde{\delta}_{Y_{2}}Q^{\text{extra}}_{Y_{1}}\,.

(263)

The last term can also be directly computed, which gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{Y_{2}}Q^{\text{extra}}_{Y_{1}}=2\oint d^{2}x\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}\Pi)_{\ell=1}U_{\ell\geq 3}\,.

(264)

Decomposing
\tilde{\delta}_{Y_{2}}Q_{Y_{1}}
as before

\tilde{\delta}_{Y_{2}}Q_{Y_{1}}=\delta_{Y_{2}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\delta_{W^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\delta_{T^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\delta_{W_{\text{log}}^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\delta_{T_{\text{log}}^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}\,,

(265)

we can can combine the third and fourth terms to get

\displaystyle\delta_{T^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\delta_{W_{\text{log}}^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}

\displaystyle=-2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{T}V_{\ell\geq 2}+2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{T})_{l\geq 2}V_{\ell\geq 2}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,[\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}]\mathcal{T}V_{\ell\geq 2}\,.

(266)

while the second and fifth terms reduce to

\displaystyle\delta_{W^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}+\delta_{T_{\text{log}}^{(Y_{2})}}Q_{Y_{1}}

\displaystyle=-2\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}}\Pi U_{\ell\geq 3}+2\oint d^{2}x\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}\Pi)_{\ell\geq 3}U_{\ell\geq 3}

\displaystyle=2\oint d^{2}x[\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}},\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}]\Pi U_{\ell\geq 3}-2\oint\mathcal{L}_{Y_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{Y_{2}}\Pi)_{\ell=1}U_{\ell\geq 3}\,.

(267)

Thus, adding all the contributions we have shown that

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{Y_{1}},\tilde{Q}_{Y_{2}}\}

\displaystyle=Q_{\hat{Y}}+Q^{\text{extra}}_{\hat{Y}}=\tilde{Q}_{\hat{Y}}\,,

(268)

where we used the original bracket
\delta_{Y_{2}}Q_{Y_{1}}=Q_{\hat{Y}}
, with

\hat{Y}^{B}=Y_{1}^{A}\partial_{A}Y^{B}_{2}-Y_{2}^{A}\partial_{A}Y^{B}_{1}\,.

(269)

•

A similar strategy allows us to compute the Poisson bracket between a Lorentz boost and a spatial rotation, obtaining

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{Y},\tilde{Q}_{b}\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{Q}_{\hat{b}}\,,\qquad\text{with}\qquad\hat{b}=Y^{A}\partial_{A}b\,,

(270)

\displaystyle\{\tilde{Q}_{b},\tilde{Q}_{Y}\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{Q}_{\hat{b}}\,,\qquad\text{with}\qquad\hat{b}=-Y^{A}\partial_{A}b\,.

(271)

We have thus shown that the new contributions to the Lorentz generators do not modify the homogeneous Lorentz subalgebra. The Poincaré algebra remains therefore untouched.

9.2.4
Alternative redefinition of the Lorentz charges

As mentioned above, there exists an alternative redefinition of the Lorentz generators (
231
), which decouples the pure BMS supertranslations from the translations, while keeping them in a non-trivial infinite-dimensional representation of the Lorentz algebra. The canonical realization for these charges can be obtained by adding a field-dependent improper gauge transformation with the following parameters

W_{\log}^{(b)}=-\Big[\frac{b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})_{\ell=1}\Big]_{\ell\geq 2}\qquad W^{(b)}=\Big[b(V)_{\ell\geq 2}\Big]_{\ell=1}\,.

(272)

This transformation leads to an extra term in the Lorentz boost charge of the form

Q^{\text{extra}}_{b}=\oint d^{2}x\Big[2b(V)_{\ell\geq 2}(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}+\pi^{rr}_{\log})_{\ell=1}\Big]\,,

(273)

while keeping the spatial rotation charge invariant. One can check that the Poisson brackets of these new Lorentz charges with pure supertranslations involve only pure supertranslations and keep the brackets with all other charges unchanged.

10
Conclusions

In this paper, we have shown that a special class of logarithmic supertranslations, characterized by definite parity properties under the antipodal map, can be consistently included in the Hamiltonian formulation of asymptotically flat spaces. One can enlarge the boundary conditions while keeping the action finite in such a way that these logarithmic supertranslations are symmetry transformations with well-defined (integrable, finite) canonical generators. We have also computed the algebra of the logarithmic supertranslation generators with the other generators of the BMS algebra (“logarithmic BMS algebra”) and found non-trivial central terms. Previous instances of central terms appearing in the asymptotic symmetry algebra occur in AdS
3
gravity
Brown:1986nw
;
Brown:1986ed
, or in five-dimensional gravity, which shares a very similar structure
Fuentealba:2021yvo
;
Fuentealba:2022yqt
.

We insisted throughout our approach both on finiteness of the symplectic form and on exact invariance of the symplectic form under the transformations of the logarithmic BMS algebra – and not just invariance up to (possibly divergent) surface terms at spatial infinity. These strong requirements lead to rather lengthy developments, but are worth being pursued for at least two reasons. (i) First, the very fact that they can be successfully implemented “on the nose”, making regularizations unnecessary, reveals non-trivial properties of the Einstein theory on asymptotically flat spaces. The detailed non-trivial cancellations underlying this success teaches us a lesson about the theory which might be less apparent in approaches where these requirements are not put in the forefront. (ii) Second, our approach guarantees that standard Hamiltionian methods can be used and, in particular, that the symmetry generators form a true (possibly non-linear) algebra under the Poisson brackets, which fulfills the Jacobi identity exactly.

Even though the logarithmic translations (
1
) are not contained in it, the class of logarithmic supertranslations that are consistently included in our approach is rather huge. Indeed, it involves one function of the angles, as do the supertranslations. The odd part of this function of the angles is related to the logarithmic supertranslations in time, while the even part is related to new supertranslations in space, which become improper with the new boundary conditions and which we also call “logarithmic supertranslations” since their presence is related to the logarithmic enlargement of the boundary conditions. As we have also shown, logarithmic supertranslations in space parametrized by the function
\tilde{U}
are proper gauge transformations with zero charge.

Furthermore, the logarithmic supertranslation charges are canonically conjugate to the pure supertranslation charges. This implies that one can generate shifts of the pure supertranslation charges by performing logarithmic supertranslations. This remarkable feature enables one to completely decouple in the asymptotic symmetry algebra pure supertranslations and logarithmic supertranslations from the Poincaré generators, without having to fix the gauge or truncating the theory.

The new Poincaré generators are invariant under supertranslations. As we have pointed out, this decoupling is conceptually very similar to the decoupling achieved in
Mirbabayi:2016axw
;
Bousso:2017dny
;
Javadinezhad:2018urv
;
Javadinezhad:2022hhl
, but our approach is entirely classical (non-quantum). The decoupling amounts to add to the Lorentz transformations appropriately chosen supertranslations and logarithmic supertranslations with coefficients that depend on the charges. These nonlinear redefinitions evade the obstructions found earlier on the impossibility to extract a natural Poincaré subalgebra through linear methods. An intermediate decoupling can be achieved, such that the brackets of the pure supertranslations with the Lorentz generators involve only the pure supertranslations and not the ordinary translations.

The logarithmic supertranslations are allowed in the formalism by including in the asymptotic form of the fields terms that take the precise form of logarithmic supertranslation variations. This is reminiscent of the method of orbits, in which one parametrizes the fields in terms of an orbit representative (which would in our case be the configuration with no
\log
-terms) and the symmetry element that brings that reference representative to the given configuration (here, the improper logarithmic diffeomorphisms). Such a presentation can in principle always be given for any symmetry.

We conclude this section with six open questions:

•

What makes the decoupling possible between the Poincaré algebra and the supertranslations is the invertible central charge appearing in the brackets of the logarithmic supertranslations with the pure supertranslations. Such a mechanism would thus be in principle available in any similar situation where such a central charge would be present. It would be interesting to investigate this mechanism in the nonlinear context of the BMS(5) algebra emerging in five spacetime dimensions
Fuentealba:2021yvo
;
Fuentealba:2022yqt
, or in the case of supersymmetry where a similar central charge also appears
Fuentealba:2021xhn
.

•

Also of interest would be to study the analogs of the logarithmic supertranslations in the Maxwell theory, which would be angle-dependent logarithmic
u(1)
gauge transformations
Henneaux:2018gfi
.

•

One might wonder if the procedure followed to include logarithmic supertranslations (i.e., introducing slowlier decaying terms in the metric that take the form of an (improper) diffeomorphism) can be pushed further to include superrotations
Banks:2003vp
;
Barnich:2009se
;
Barnich:2010eb
or diffeomorphisms of the sphere

Campiglia:2014yka
;
Campiglia:2015yka
, which would need
\mathcal{O}(1)
deviations from the flat metric. The answer to this question is not immediate, precisely because the allowed new terms would not be proper gauge transformations but would be (if successfully included) improper gauge ones. In that context, adding new surface degrees of freedom might perhaps help for finiteness or integrability of the charges
Henneaux:2018gfi
.

•

Since our new boundary conditions allow logarithmic supertranslations, a natural question is: what are the Ward identities associated with these new symmetries
Strominger:2013jfa
;
He:2014laa
;
Strominger:2017zoo
? Furthermore, in view of the decoupling mechanism, one might wonder whether these Ward identities would provide new insightful information on “hard processes”, or merely constraint “soft processes”, see
Mirbabayi:2016axw
;
Bousso:2017dny
;
Javadinezhad:2018urv
;
Javadinezhad:2022hhl
.

•

In the same vein, one would like to repeat the complete analysis at null infinity and, in particular, write down the action of the logarithmic supertranslations there and study their matching with logarithmic supertranslations at spatial infinity along the lines of
Troessaert:2017jcm
;
Henneaux:2018hdj
.

•

Finally we point out that our boundary conditions yield a Weyl tensor which contains no log-type singularity as one integrates the equations to null infinity
Henneaux:2018hdj
;
Fried1
;
Friedrich:1999wk
;
Friedrich:1999ax
;
Mohamed:2021rfg
, since the boundary conditions differ from those of
Henneaux:2018hdj
or
Regge:1974zd
only by diffeomorphism terms to which the Weyl tensor is blind
7
7

7

In fact, the regularity and parity conditions on the Weyl tensor imply the boundary conditions given in this paper if one allows
\log
-terms in the asymptotic expansion of the metric. This is an extension of the results of
Henneaux:2018hdj
.
. In that respect, it would be of interest to extend the analysis to the alternative BMS-invariant boundary conditions of
Henneaux:2018cst
, which do generically lead to log-type singularities at null infinity. A motivation for achieving this task is given by
Winicour1
.

Work along these lines is currently in progress.

Notes added

After our paper was completed, the reference
Javadinezhad:2022ldc
was posted on the archive. That work studies how to redefine angular momentum flux at null infinity in order to make it free from supertranslation ambiguities. It would be interesting to study the connection with our construction.

We should also mention the existence of similarities of our symmetry structure with the direct product structure of the symmetry algebra derived in
Adami:2021nnf
on null boundaries. We thank Shahin Sheikh-Jabbari for a discussion on this point.

Appendices

Appendix A
2+1
Decomposition of the spatial metric and spatial curvature

This appendix provides useful formulas related to the
2+1
slicing of the spatial equal time hypersurfaces by spheres of constant radius
r
.
The “lapse” is denoted
\lambda

while the “shift” is
\lambda^{A}
,

\gamma_{AB}\equiv g_{AB},\quad\lambda_{A}\equiv g_{rA},\quad\lambda\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{g^{rr}}}.

(274)

The three-dimensional spatial metric and its inverse
take the form

g_{ij}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\lambda^{2}+\lambda_{C}\lambda^{C}&\lambda_{B}\\
\lambda_{A}&\gamma_{AB}\end{array}\right),\quad g^{ij}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}&-\frac{\lambda^{B}}{\lambda^{2}}\\
-\frac{\lambda^{A}}{\lambda^{2}}&\gamma^{AB}+\frac{\lambda^{A}\lambda^{B}}{\lambda^{2}}\end{array}\right)\,.

(275)

Here, the
2
-dimensional metric
\gamma_{AB}
and its inverse
\gamma^{AB}
have been used to
raise and lower the angular indices
A,B,...
. The Christoffel symbols
can be written in terms of the extrinsic curvature of the spheres

K_{AB}
:

\displaystyle K_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(-\partial_{r}g_{AB}+D_{A}\lambda_{B}+D_{B}\lambda_{A}\right)\,,

(276)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{AB}^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}K_{AB}\,,

(277)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{BC}^{A}

\displaystyle=

{}^{\gamma}\Gamma_{BC}^{A}-\frac{\lambda^{A}}{\lambda}K_{BC}\,,

(278)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{rA}^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\partial_{A}\lambda+K_{AB}\lambda^{B}\right)\,,

(279)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{rr}^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\partial_{r}\lambda+\frac{\lambda^{A}}{\lambda}\left(\partial_{A}\lambda+K_{AB}\lambda^{B}\right)\,,

(280)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{rB}^{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda^{A}}{\lambda}\left(\partial_{B}\lambda+K_{BC}\lambda^{C}\right)+D_{B}\lambda^{A}-\lambda K_{B}^{A}\,,

(281)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{rr}^{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\lambda\left(\gamma^{AB}+\frac{\lambda^{A}\lambda^{B}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\left(\partial_{B}\lambda+K_{BC}\lambda^{C}\right)-\lambda^{C}\left(D^{A}\lambda_{C}-\lambda K_{C}^{A}\right)

(282)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\lambda^{A}}{\lambda}\partial_{r}\lambda+\gamma^{AB}\partial_{r}\lambda_{B}\,.

D_{A}
denotes the covariant derivative associated to
\gamma_{AB}
. The
components of the Ricci tensor can be obtained from

{}^{(3)}R_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\partial_{r}K_{AB}+2K_{AC}K_{B}^{C}-KK_{AB}-\frac{1}{\lambda}D_{A}D_{B}\lambda

(283)

\displaystyle+^{\gamma}R_{AB}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}K_{AB}\,,

{}^{(3)}R_{rA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\partial_{A}K-D_{B}K_{A}^{B}\right)+{}^{(3)}R_{AB}\lambda^{B}\,,

(284)

{}^{(3)}R_{rr}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\partial_{r}K-\lambda^{A}\partial_{A}K)-\lambda^{2}K_{B}^{A}K_{A}^{B}-\lambda D_{A}D^{A}\lambda

(285)

\displaystyle-^{(3)}R_{AB}\lambda^{A}\lambda^{B}+2\,{}^{(3)}R_{rB}\lambda^{B}\,,

which implies the expression

{}^{(3)}R=\frac{2}{\lambda}(\partial_{r}K-\lambda^{A}\partial_{A}K)+{}^{\gamma}R-K_{B}^{A}K_{A}^{B}-K^{2}-\frac{2}{\lambda}D_{A}D^{A}\lambda\,

(286)

for the Ricci scalar.

Appendix B
Vanishing of the logarithmic divergences in the boost
generator

In this appendix we show that the remaining divergences (proportional to
\ln^{2}r

and
\ln r
) in the boost generator vanish. These are, taking into account that many terms obviously vanish due to parity properties,

\delta Q_{\log}^{(2)}=\ln^{2}r\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\delta\left(2\theta^{(2)}+2k_{\log(2)}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{4}\theta^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)\,,

(287)

and

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\log}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\ln r\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[b\Big(2\delta\sigma+2\delta k_{\log(1)}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{2}\delta(-3\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}+h\theta)+\frac{1}{4}\delta(3\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-\theta^{2})

\displaystyle\quad-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\delta\theta+\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\delta\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big)-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\theta+4b\delta\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{V}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\Big)\Big]\,,

(288)

(the term proportional to
\tilde{T}
in
Q_{\log}
in (
186
) is manifestly zero as follows from similar parity considerations).

The critical tool in establishing the absence of divergences is the fast decay of the constraints imposed in Section
4
.

•

Let us first focus on the
\ln^{2}r
-divergent term, which reads

\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\delta\left(2\theta^{(2)}+2k_{\log(2)}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{4}\theta^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}\right)\,.

(289)

We can see that the quadratic terms in
\theta_{AB}
vanish under
the integral because of parity (recall that
\theta_{AB}
is even).
Then, we must only deal with the integral

\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\left(\theta^{(2)}+k_{\log(2)}^{(2)}\right)\,.

(290)

We now make use of the condition
\mathcal{H}_{\log(1)}=0
in (
44
),
which allows to relate
\theta^{(2)}
with other coefficients of
the fall-off of the fields. Thus, we find that

\displaystyle\theta^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\Big[4k_{\log(2)}^{(2)}-2h_{rr}^{\log(1)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h_{rr}^{\log(1)}-\sigma+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\sigma_{AB}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\Big(-3\theta_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}+\theta^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}h_{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}

\displaystyle\quad-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{C}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\pi_{\log}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\pi_{\log}^{rr}+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\pi_{\log A}^{r}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\pi_{\log A}^{B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\pi_{\log}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\pi_{\log}\Big)\,.

(291)

Then, the integral in (
290
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\left[2h_{rr}^{\log(1)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h_{rr}^{\log(1)}+\sigma-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\sigma_{AB}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\Big(3\theta_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\theta^{2}\Big)\right]

(292)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\frac{b}{\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\pi_{\log}^{rr}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\pi_{\log}^{rr}+4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rA}\pi_{\log A}^{r}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\pi_{\log A}^{B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{rr}\pi_{\log}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\pi_{\log}\Big)

(293)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\Big(\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\theta-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}h_{rr}

\displaystyle\qquad-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}

\displaystyle\qquad+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{C}\Big)\,.

(294)

It is easy to show that the integral in (
292
) is zero
by virtue of the equation for the boost parameter
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}b+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}b=0

(after integrating by parts) and the fact that
\theta_{AB}
is even
and
b
is odd.

For the integral in (
293
), we can see that only the “pure
gauge part” of the subleading terms in the asymptotic expansion of the conjugate momentum, i.e.,

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{rr}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,V_{\text{even}\,,}

(295)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{odd}}^{rA}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V_{\text{even}}\,,

(296)

\displaystyle\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\pi$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{\text{even}}^{AB}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}V_{\text{even}}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}V_{\text{even}})\,,

(297)

contributes
to the surface integral.
Thus, (
293
) reduces to

\oint d^{2}xb\left(-4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}V\pi_{\log}^{rA}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}V\pi_{\log}^{AB}\right)\,.

(298)

If we integrate
by parts, we get

\oint d^{2}x\Big[4\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}b(\pi^{rA}_{\text{log}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi^{AB}_{\text{log}})+2b(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi^{AB}_{\text{log}}-\pi_{\text{log}}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi^{rA}_{\text{log}})\Big]\,,

(299)

where the equation for the boost parameter was used. If we now consider the asymptotic constraint equations
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\pi_{\log}^{AB}+\pi_{\log}^{rA}=0

and
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\pi_{\log}^{AB}+\pi_{\log}=0
, the above
integral is straightforwardly seen to vanish.

Accordingly, it only remains to show that (
294
) is zero.
Again parity considerations imply that (
294
) reduces to

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\Big(\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\theta_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}

(300)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}-\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{C}+\theta_{B}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}+\frac{1}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{C}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}^{C}\Big)\,,

(301)

where only the pure gauge part of

(odd) contributes,

\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}^{\text{odd}}=2\left(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}U_{\text{odd}}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{AB}U_{\text{odd}}\right)\,.

(302)

Integrating by parts we get that (

) becomes

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,U_{\text{odd}}\Big[

\displaystyle b\Big(2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\theta_{B}^{A}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}

\displaystyle+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}\Big)+\partial_{A}b\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\theta-2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\theta_{C}^{B}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{C}\theta_{C}^{B}

(303)

Using that
\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\theta_{BC}=\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}\theta_{AC}

•

We will now show that the
\ln r
-divergent term

\displaystyle\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\Big[b\Big(2\delta\sigma+2\delta k_{\log(1)}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{2}\delta(-3\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}+h\theta)-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{4}\delta(3\theta_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}-\theta^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\theta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}-2\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}-\delta\theta_{AB}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{B}\Big)-\partial_{A}b\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\lambda$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\delta\theta-4b\delta\Big(\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}\tilde{V}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}V\Big)\Big]

(306)

is also zero. Parity conditions of the fields imply that the above integral
reduces to

\oint d^{2}x\sqrt{\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$g$\kern 0.0pt}}}}\,b\delta\Big(2\sigma+2k_{\log(1)}^{(2)}-\frac{3}{2}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{A}^{B}\theta_{B}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}h\theta\Big)\,.

The condition

in (

) allows one to obtain an expression
for

in terms of the other asymptotic coefficients in the decay of the fields

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(2k_{\log(1)}^{(2)}-2h_{rr}^{(2)}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h_{rr}^{(2)}-h^{(2)}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}^{A}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$D$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{B}h_{A}^{(2)B}-\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,h^{(2)}+2\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}^{2}+\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$\triangle$\kern 0.0pt}}}\,\hbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.5pt\kern 1.29167pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt$h$\kern 0.0pt}}}_{rr}
